When this occurs,\n        subtracts the lattice vector in this direction.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        shape : array-like\n            The x, y, z distances of the periodic boundary.\n        '''\n\n        dx, dy, dz = shape\n        points = self.points\n        for i in range(1, len(points)):\n            prevLine = points[i-1]\n            curLine = points[i]\n            rest = points[i:]\n            change = curLine - prevLine\n            if -1.05*dx < change[0] < -0.95*dx:\n                rest[:, 0] += dx\n            if 1.05*dx > change[0] > 0.95*dx:\n                rest[:, 0] -= dx\n            if -1.05*dy < change[1] < -0.95*dy:\n                rest[:, 1] += dy\n            if 1.05*dy > change[1] > 0.95*dy:\n                rest[:, 1] -= dy\n            if -1.05*dz < change[2] < -0.95*dz:\n                rest[:, 2] += dz\n            if 1.05*dz > change[2] > 0.95*dz:\n                rest[:, 2] -= dz\n\n        self.points = points\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_gauss_code(cls, code):\n        '''Creates a Knot from the given code, which must be provided as a\n        string and may optionally include crossing orientations (these are\n        actually ignored).'''\n        from pyknotid.representations import Representation\n        if 'a' in code or 'c' in code:\n            k = cls(Representation(code).space_curve())\n        else:\n            k = cls(Representation.calculating_orientations(code).space_curve())\n        k.rotate((0.05, 0.03, 0.02))\n        return k\n\n    @classmethod\n    def closing_on_sphere(cls, line, com=(0., 0., 0.)):\n        '''Adds new vertices to close the line at its maximum radius,\n        returning a SpaceCurve representing the result.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        line : ndarray\n            The points of the line.\n        com : iterable\n            Optional additional centre of mass to shift by before closing\n\n        '''\n        com = ensure_shape_tuple(com)\n\n        points = line - n.array(com)\n\n        start = points[0]\n        end = points[-1]\n\n        start_r = mag(start)\n        end_r = mag(end)\n\n        start_theta = n.arccos(start[2] / start_r)\n        end_theta = n.arccos(end[2] / end_r)\n\n        start_phi = n.arctan2(start[1], start[0])\n        end_phi = n.arctan2(end[1], end[0])\n\n        rs = n.linspace(end_r, start_r, 100)\n        thetas = n.linspace(end_theta, start_theta, 100)\n        phis = n.linspace(end_phi, start_phi, 100)\n\n        join_points = n.zeros((100, 3))\n        join_points[:, 2] = rs * n.cos(thetas)\n        join_points[:, 0] = rs * n.sin(thetas) * n.cos(phis)\n        join_points[:, 1] = rs * n.sin(thetas) * n.sin(phis)\n\n        return cls(n.vstack((points, join_points[1:-1])) + n.array(com))\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_periodic_line(cls, line, shape, perturb=True, **kwargs):\n        '''Returns a :class:`SpaceCurve` instance in which the line has been\n        unwrapped through\n        the periodic boundaries.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        line : array-like\n            The Nx3 vector of points in the line\n        shape : array-like\n            The x, y, z distances of the periodic boundary\n        perturb : bool\n            If True, translates and rotates the knot to avoid any lattice\n            problems.\n        '''\n        shape = ensure_shape_tuple(shape)\n        knot = cls(line, **kwargs)\n        knot._unwrap_periodicity(shape)\n        if perturb:\n            knot.translate(n.array([0.00123, 0.00231, 0.00321]))\n            knot.rotate((0.0002, 0.0001, 0.0001))\n        return knot\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_lattice_data(cls, line):\n        '''Returns a :class:`SpaceCurve` instance in which the line has been\n        slightly translated and rotated, in order to (practically) ensure\n        no self intersections in closure or coincident points in\n        projection.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        line : array-like\n            The list of points in the line. May be any type that SpaceCurve\n            normally accepts.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        :class:`SpaceCurve`\n        '''\n        knot = cls(line)\n        knot.translate(n.array([0.00123, 0.00231, 0.00321]))\n        knot.rotate(n.random.random(3) * 0.012)\n        return knot\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_braid_word(cls, word):\n        '''\n        Returns a :class:`SpaceCurve` instance formed from the given braid\n        word.\n\n        The braid word should be of the form 'aAbBcC' (i.e. capitalisation\n        denotes inverse).\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        word : str\n            The braid word to interpret.\n        '''\n\n        num_strands = len(set(word.lower())) + 1\n\n        xs = n.arange(num_strands) + 1\n\n        y = 0.\n\n        lines = [[[x, y, 0.]] for x in xs]\n\n        strands_by_final_index = {strand: lines[strand] for strand in range(num_strands)}\n        strands_by_initial_index = {strand: lines[strand] for strand in range(num_strands)}\n\n        for letter in word:\n            index = ord(letter.lower()) - ord('a')\n\n            sign = '+' if letter.isupper() else '-'\n\n            if sign == '+':\n                lines[index].append([index + 1 + 0.5, y + 0.5, 1.])\n                lines[index].append([index + 1 + 1., y + 1., 0.])\n                lines[index + 1].append([index + 1 + 0.5, y + 0.5, -1.])\n                lines[index + 1].append([index + 1, y + 1., 0.])\n            else:\n                lines[index + 1].append([index + 1 + 0.5, y + 0.5, 1.])\n                lines[index + 1].append([index + 1, y + 1., 0.])\n                lines[index].append([index + 1 + 0.5, y + 0.5, -1.])\n                lines[index].append([index + 1 + 1., y + 1., 0.])\n\n            lines[index], lines[index + 1] = lines[index + 1], lines[index]\n            strands_by_final_index[index], strands_by_final_index[index + 1] = strands_by_final_index[index + 1], strands_by_final_index[index]\n\n            y += 1.\n\n            for i, line in enumerate(lines):\n                if i not in (index, index + 1):\n                    lines[i].append([i + 1., y, 0.])\n\n        for i, line in enumerate(lines):\n            line.append([0., y + i + 1, 0.])\n            line.append([-1*(i + 1), y, 0.])\n            line.append([-1*(i + 1), 0, 0.])\n            line.append([0., -i - 1., 0.])\n            line.append([i + 1., -0.5, 0.])\n\n        # indices_by_strand = {strand: index for (strand, index) in strands_by_final_index.items()}\n\n        line = []\n        index = 0\n        for i in range(num_strands):\n            cur_strand = strands_by_initial_index[index]\n            line.extend(cur_strand)\n            print('index is', index)\n            print('cur strand is', cur_strand)\n            index = int(np.round(cur_strand[-1][0])) - 1\n\n        k = cls(n.array(line)*5.)\n        k.zero_centroid()\n        return k\n\n    def scale(self, factor):\n        '''Scales all the points of self by the given factor.\n\n        You can accomplish the same thing, or other more subtle\n        transformations, by modifying self.:py:attr:`points`.\n        '''\n        self.points *= factor\n\n    def translate(self, vector):\n\n        '''Translates all the points of self by the given vector.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        vector : array-like\n            The x, y, z translation distances\n        '''\n        self.points = self.points + n.array(vector)\n\n    def zero_centroid(self):\n        '''\n        Translate such that the centroid (average position of vertices)\n        is at (0, 0, 0).\n        '''\n        centroid = n.average(self.points, axis=0)\n        self.translate(-1*centroid)\n\n    def rotate(self, angles=None):\n        '''\n        Rotates all the points of self by the given angles in each axis.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        angles : array-like\n            The rotation angles about x, y and z. If None, random\n            angles are used. Defaults to None.\n        '''\n        if angles is None:\n            angles = n.random.random(3)\n        phi, theta, psi = angles\n        rot_mat = get_rotation_matrix(angles)\n        self._apply_matrix(rot_mat)\n\n    def _apply_matrix(self, mat):\n        '''\n        Applies the given matrix to all of self.points.\n        '''\n        self.points = n.apply_along_axis(mat.dot, 1, self.points)\n\n    def cuaps(self, include_closure=True):\n        '''Returns a list of the 'cuaps', where the curve is parallel to the\n        positive x-axis. See D Bar-Natan and R van der Veen, \"A\n        polynomial time knot polynomial\", 2017.\n        '''\n        points = self.points\n        if mag(points[-1] - points[0]) < 0.000001:\n            points = points[:-1]  # Avoids a badly defined tangent\n        tangents = np.roll(points, -1, axis=0) - points\n        angles = np.arctan2(tangents[:, 1], tangents[:, 0])\n\n        print('angles', angles)\n\n        cuaps = []\n\n        for i in range(len(points) - 2):\n            angle = angles[i]\n            next_angle = angles[i + 1]\n\n            if (0 < angle < np.pi / 2.) and (-np.pi / 2. < next_angle < 0):\n                cuaps.append([i + 1, -1])\n            elif (-np.pi / 2. < angle < 0) and (0 < next_angle < np.pi / 2.):\n                cuaps.append([i + 1, 1])\n\n        if include_closure:\n            angle = angles[-2]\n            next_angle = angles[-1]\n            if (0 < angle < np.pi / 2.) and (-np.pi / 2. < next_angle < 0):\n                cuaps.append([len(angles) - 1, -1])\n            elif (-np.pi / 2. < angle < 0) and (0 < next_angle < np.pi / 2.):\n                cuaps.append([len(angles) - 1, 1])\n\n            angle = angles[-1]\n            next_angle = angles[0]\n            if (0 < angle < np.pi / 2.) and (-np.pi / 2. < next_angle < 0):\n                cuaps.append([0, -1])\n            elif (-np.pi / 2. < angle < 0) and (0 < next_angle < np.pi / 2.):\n                cuaps.append([0, 1])\n\n        cuaps = np.array(cuaps)\n        # cuaps[:, -1] *= -1\n\n        return cuaps\n\n    def raw_crossings(self, mode='use_max_jump', include_closure=True,\n                      recalculate=False, try_cython=True):\n\n        '''Returns the crossings in the diagram of the projection of the\n        space curve into its z=0 plane.\n\n        The crossings will be calculated the first time this function\n        is called, then cached until an operation that would change\n        the list (e.g. rotation, or changing ``self.points``).\n\n        Multiple modes are available (see parameters) - you should be\n        aware of this because different modes may be vastly slower or\n        faster depending on the type of line.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        mode : str, optional\n            One of ``'count_every_jump'`` or ``'use_max_jump'`` or\n            ``'naive'``. In the first case,\n            walking along the line uses information about the length of\n            every step. In the second, it guesses that all steps have the\n            same length as the maximum step length. In the last, no\n            optimisation is made and every crossing is checked.\n            The optimal choice depends on the data but is usually\n            ``'use_max_jump'``, which is the default.\n        include_closure : bool, optional\n            Whether to include crossings with the\n            line joining the start and end points. Defaults to True.\n        recalculate : bool, optional\n            Whether to force a recalculation of the crossing positions.\n            Defaults to False.\n        try_cython : bool, optional\n            Whether to force the use of the python (as opposed to cython)\n            implementation of find_crossings. This will make no difference\n            if the cython could not be loaded, in which case python is already\n            used automatically. Defaults to True.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        array-like\n            The raw array of floats representing crossings, of the\n            form [[line_index, other_index, +-1, +-1], ...], where the\n            line_index and other_index are in arclength parameterised\n            by integers for each vertex and linearly interpolated,\n            and the +-1 represent over/under and clockwise/anticlockwise\n            respectively.\n        '''\n\n        if not recalculate and self._crossings is not None:\n            return self._crossings\n\n        if try_cython:\n            helpers_module = chelpers\n        else:\n            helpers_module = helpers\n\n        self._vprint('Finding crossings')\n\n        points = self.points\n        segment_lengths = n.roll(points[:, :2], -1, axis=0) - points[:, :2]\n        segment_lengths = n.sqrt(n.sum(segment_lengths * segment_lengths,\n                                       axis=1))\n        # if include_closure:\n        #     max_segment_length = n.max(segment_lengths)\n        # else:\n        max_segment_length = n.max(segment_lengths[:-1])\n\n        numtries = len(points) - 3\n\n        crossings = []\n\n        jump_mode = {'count_every_jump': 1, 'use_max_jump': 2,\n                     'naive': 3}[mode]\n\n        for i in range(len(points)-2):\n            if self.verbose:\n                if i % 100 == 0:\n                    self._vprint('\\ri = {} / {}'.format(i, numtries),\n                                 False)\n            v0 = points[i]\n            dv = points[(i+1) % len(points)] - v0\n\n            s = points[i+2:]\n            vnum = i\n            compnum = i+2\n\n            crossings.extend(helpers_module.find_crossings(\n                v0, dv, s, segment_lengths[compnum:],\n                vnum, compnum,\n                max_segment_length,\n                jump_mode\n                ))\n\n        if include_closure:\n            closure_segment_length = segment_lengths[-1]\n            max_segment_length = n.max([closure_segment_length,\n                                        max_segment_length])\n            v0 = points[-1]\n            dv = points[0] - v0\n            s = points[1:-1]\n            vnum = len(points) - 1\n            compnum = 1\n            crossings.extend(helpers_module.find_crossings(\n                v0, dv, s, segment_lengths[compnum:],\n                vnum, compnum,\n                max_segment_length,\n                jump_mode))\n\n        self._vprint('\\n{} crossings found\\n'.format(len(crossings) / 2))\n        crossings.sort(key=lambda s: s[0])\n        crossings = n.array(crossings)\n        self._crossings = crossings\n\n        return crossings\n\n    def planar_writhe(self, **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Returns the current planar writhe of the knot; the signed sum\n        of crossings of the current projection.\n\n        The 'true' writhe is the average of this quantity over all\n        projection directions, and is available from the :meth:`writhe`\n        method.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        **kwargs :\n            These are passed directly to :meth:`raw_crossings`.\n        '''\n        crossings = self.raw_crossings(**kwargs)\n        return n.sum(crossings[:, 3]) / 2.\n\n    def distance_quantity(self):\n        from pyknotid.spacecurves.complexity import distance_quantity\n        return distance_quantity(self.points)\n\n    def writhe(self, samples=10, recalculate=False, method='integral',\n               include_acn=False, **kwargs):\n        '''\n        The (approximate) writhe of the space curve, obtained by averaging\n        the planar writhe over the given number of directions.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        samples : int\n            The number of directions to average over. Defaults to 10.\n        recalculate : bool\n            Whether to recalculate the writhe even if a cached result\n            is available. Defaults to False.\n        method : str\n            If 'projections', averages the planar writhe over many\n            projections. If 'integral', calculates the writhing integral.\n        **kwargs :\n            These are passed directly to :meth:`raw_crossings`.\n        '''\n        if method == 'projections':\n            acn, writhe = self._writhe_and_crossing_numbers(\n                samples, recalculate=recalculate, **kwargs)\n\n        elif method == 'integral':\n            from pyknotid.spacecurves.complexity import writhe_and_acn_integral\n            writhe, acn = writhe_and_acn_integral(self.points, **kwargs)\n\n        if include_acn:\n            return writhe, acn\n        return writhe\n\n    def higher_order_writhe(self, order=(1, 3, 2, 4), try_cython=True):\n        from pyknotid.spacecurves.complexity import higher_order_writhe_integral\n        return higher_order_writhe_integral(self.points, order=order,\n                                            try_cython=try_cython)\n\n    def second_order_writhes(self, try_cython=True, **kwargs):\n        from pyknotid.spacecurves.complexity import second_order_writhes\n        return second_order_writhes(self.points, try_cython=try_cython, **kwargs)\n\n    def planar_second_order_writhe(self, **kwargs):\n        '''The second order writhe (type 2, i1,i3,i2,i4) of the projection of\n        the curve along the z axis.\n        '''\n        from pyknotid.invariants import second_order_writhe\n        gc = self.gauss_code(**kwargs)\n        return second_order_writhe(gc)\n\n    def second_order_twist(self, z):\n        from pyknotid.spacecurves import complexity as com\n        z = np.array(z).astype(np.float64)\n        assert len(z) == 3\n        return com.second_order_twist(self.points, z)\n\n    def average_crossing_number(self, samples=10, recalculate=False,\n                                **kwargs):\n        '''\n        The (approximate) average crossing number of the space curve,\n        obtained by averaging\n        the planar writhe over the given number of directions.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        samples : int\n            The number of directions to average over.\n        recalculate : bool\n            Whether to recalculate the ACN.\n        **kwargs :\n            These are passed directly to :meth:`raw_crossings`.\n        '''\n        crossing_number, writhe = self._writhe_and_crossing_numbers(\n            samples, recalculate=recalculate, **kwargs)\n        return crossing_number\n\n    def _writhe_and_crossing_numbers(self, samples=10, recalculate=False,\n                                     include_closure=False,\n                                     **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Calculates and stores the writhe and average crossing number.\n        Internal (not intended for external use).\n        '''\n        if (self._cached_writhe_and_crossing_numbers is not None and\n            self._cached_writhe_and_crossing_numbers[0] == samples and\n            not recalculate):\n            return self._cached_writhe_and_crossing_numbers[1]\n\n        from .complexity import writhe_and_crossing_number\n        numbers = writhe_and_crossing_number(self.points, samples,\n                                             verbose=self.verbose,\n                                             include_closure=include_closure,\n                                             **kwargs)\n        self._cached_writhe_and_crossing_numbers = (samples, numbers)\n\n        return numbers\n\n    def gauss_code(self, recalculate=False, **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Returns a :class:`~pyknotid.representations.gausscode.GaussCode`\n        instance representing the crossings of the knot.\n\n        The GaussCode instance is cached internally. If you want to\n        recalculate it (e.g. to get an unsimplified version if you\n        have simplified it), you should pass `recalculate=True`.\n\n        This method passes kwargs directly to :meth:`raw_crossings`,\n        see the documentation of that function for all options.\n        '''\n\n        from ..representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n        if not recalculate and self._gauss_code is not None:\n            return self._gauss_code\n        crossings = self.raw_crossings(recalculate=recalculate, **kwargs)\n        gc = GaussCode(crossings, verbose=self.verbose)\n        self._gauss_code = gc\n        return gc\n\n    def representation(self, recalculate=False, **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Returns a :class:`~pyknotid.representations.representation.Representation`\n        instance representing the crossings of the knot.\n\n        The Representation instance is cached internally. If you want to\n        recalculate it (e.g. to get an unsimplified version if you\n        have simplified it), you should pass `recalculate=True`.\n\n        This method passes kwargs directly to :meth:`raw_crossings`,\n        see the documentation of that function for all options.\n        '''\n\n        from ..representations.representation import Representation\n        if not recalculate and self._representation is not None:\n            return self._representation\n        crossings = self.raw_crossings(recalculate=recalculate, **kwargs)\n        gc = Representation(crossings, verbose=self.verbose)\n        self._representation = gc\n        return gc\n        \n\n    def planar_diagram(self, **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Returns a\n        :class:`~pyknotid.representations.planardiagram.PlanarDiagram`\n        instance representing the crossings of the knot.\n\n        This method passes kwargs directly to :meth:`raw_crossings`,\n        see the documentation of that function for all options.\n        '''\n        from ..representations.planardiagram import PlanarDiagram\n        crossings = self.raw_crossings(**kwargs)\n        return PlanarDiagram(crossings)\n\n    def gauss_diagram(self, simplify=False, **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Returns a\n        :class:`~pyknotid.representations.gaussdiagram.GaussDiagram`\n        instance representing the crossings of the knot.\n\n        This method passes kwargs directly to :meth:`raw_crossings`,\n        see the documentation of that function for all options.\n        '''\n        from ..representations.gaussdiagram import GaussDiagram\n        gc = self.gauss_code(**kwargs)\n        if simplify:\n            gc.simplify()\n        return GaussDiagram(gc)\n\n    def reconstructed_space_curve(self):\n        r = self.representation()\n        return SpaceCurve(r.space_curve())\n\n    def plot(self, mode='auto', clf=True, closed=False,\n             **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Plots the line. See :func:`pyknotid.visualise.plot_line` for\n        full documentation.\n        '''\n        plot_line(self.points, mode=mode, clf=clf,\n                  closed=closed, **kwargs)\n\n    def plot_projection(self, with_crossings=True, mark_start=False,\n                        fig_ax=None, show=True, mark_points=False):\n        '''Plots a 2D diagram of the knot projected along the current\n        z-axis. The crossings, and start point of the curve, can\n        optionally be marked.\n\n        The projection is drawn using matplotlib.\n\n        .. image:: example_knot_projection_9_5.png\n           :align: center\n           :scale: 50%\n           :alt: An example knot projection.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        with_crossings : bool\n            If True, marks the location of each self-intersection in\n            projection. Defaults to True.\n        mark_start : bool\n            If True, marks the first point of the curve. Default to False.\n        fig_ax : tuple\n            A 2-tuple of the matplotlib (fig, ax) to use, or None\n            to create a new pair.\n        show : bool\n            If True, opens a new window showing the drawing. Defaults\n            to True.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        A 2-tuple of the matplotlib (fig, ax) used for the drawing.\n        '''\n        points = self.points\n        crossings = None\n        plot_crossings = []\n        if with_crossings:\n            crossings = self.raw_crossings()\n            plot_crossings = []\n            for crossing in crossings:\n                x, y, over, orientation = crossing\n                xint = int(x)\n                r = points[xint]\n                dr = points[(xint+1) % len(points)] - r\n                plot_crossings.append(r + (x-xint) * dr)\n        fig, ax = plot_projection(points,\n                                  crossings=n.array(plot_crossings),\n                                  mark_start=mark_start,\n                                  mark_points=mark_points,\n                                  fig_ax=fig_ax,\n                                  show=show)\n        return fig, ax\n\n    def __str__(self):\n        if self._crossings is not None:\n            return '<SpaceCurve with {} points, {} crossings>'.format(\n                len(self.points), len(self._crossings))\n        return '<SpaceCurve with {} points>'.format(len(self.points))\n\n    def __repr__(self):\n        return str(self)\n\n    def to_json(self, filen):\n        '''\n        Writes the knot points to the given filename, in a json format\n        that can be read later by :meth:`SpaceCurve.from_json`. Uses\n        :func:`pyknotid.io.to_json_file` internally.\n        '''\n        to_json_file(self.points, filen)\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_json(cls, filen):\n        '''\n        Loads knot points from the given filename, assuming json format,\n        and returns a :class:`SpaceCurve` with those points.\n        '''\n\n        points = from_json_file(filen)\n        return cls(points)\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_csv(cls, filen, **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Loads knot points from the given csv file, and returns a\n        :class:`SpaceCurve` with those points.\n        \n        Arguments are passed straight to :func:`pyknot.io.from_csv`.\n        '''\n        return cls(from_csv(filen, **kwargs))\n\n    def to_txt(self, filen):\n        '''\n        Writes the knot points to the given filename, formatted\n        with each x,y,z component of each point space-separated\n        on its own line, i.e.::\n\n            ...\n            1.2 6.1 98.5\n            6.19 8.5 1.9\n            ...\n        '''\n        with open(filen, 'w') as fileh:\n            for line in self.points:\n                fileh.write('{} {} {}\\n'.format(line[0], line[1], line[2]))\n\n    def octree_simplify(self, runs=1, plot=False, rotate=True,\n                        obey_knotting=True, **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Simplifies the curve via the octree reduction of\n        :module:`pyknotid.simplify.octree`.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        runs : int\n            The number of times to run the octree simplification.\n            Defaults to 1.\n        plot : bool\n            Whether to plot the curve after each run. Defaults to False.\n        rotate : bool\n            Whether to rotate the space curve before each run. Defaults\n            to True as this can make things much faster.\n        obey_knotting : bool\n            Whether to not let the line pass through itself. 1) % len(points)\n            arclength_below = cumulative_arclength[last_lower_index]\n            step_arclength = segment_arclengths[last_lower_index]\n            step_fraction = (arclength - arclength_below) / step_arclength\n            indices.append(last_lower_index + step_fraction)\n\n        return indices\n\n    def segment_arclengths(self):\n        '''\n        Returns an array of arclengths of every step in the line\n        defined by self.points.\n        '''\n        return n.apply_along_axis(\n            mag, 1, n.roll(self.points, -1, axis=0) - For topologically\n                     safe reduction, see :meth:`octree_simplify`.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        repeats : int\n            Number of times to run the smoothing algorithm. 10e-10):\n                keep_points[i1 + 1] = 0\n\n        self.points = self.points[keep_points]\n\n    def curvatures(self, closed=True):\n        '''Returns curvatures at each vertex (or really line segment).'''\n        points = self.points\n\n        ts = np.roll(points, -1, axis=0) - points\n        vs = np.sqrt(np.sum(ts*ts, axis=1))\n\n        gamma_dd = 0.25 * (np.roll(points, -2, axis=0) + np.roll(points, 2, axis=0) - 2*points)\n        vns = 0.5 * (vs + np.roll(vs, 1))\n        gamma_d = 0.5 * (np.roll(points, -1, axis=0) - np.roll(points, 1, axis=0))\n        ans = 0.25 * (np.roll(vs, -1) + vs - np.roll(vs, 1) - np.roll(vs, 2))\n        kappas = gamma_dd - np.einsum('i,ij->ij', (ans / vns), gamma_d)\n        kappas = np.sqrt(np.sum(kappas * kappas, axis=1))\n        kappas = (1./vns**2) * kappas\n\n        if not closed:\n            kappas[:3] = kappas[3]\n            kappas[-3:] = kappas[-4]\n\n        return kappas\n\n    def sum_of_angles(self):\n        points = self.points\n        segments = np.roll(points, -1, axis=0) - points\n        segments_2 = np.roll(segments, -1, axis=0)\n\n        mags = np.sqrt(np.sum(segments * segments, axis=1))\n        mags_2 = np.roll(mags, -1)\n\n        angles = np.arccos(np.sum(segments * segments_2, axis=1) / (mags * mags_2))\n        return np.sum(angles)\n\n\n    def torsions(self, signed=False, closed=True):\n        '''Returns torsions at each vertex.'''\n\n        d = self.points\n\n        ts = np.roll(d, -1, axis=0) - np.roll(d, 1, axis=0))\n        ans = 0.25 * (np.roll(vs, -1) + vs - np.roll(vs, 1) - np.roll(vs, 2))\n\n        kappas = gamma_dd - np.einsum('i,ij->ij', (ans / vns), gamma_d)\n        kappas = np.sum(kappas * kappas, axis=1)\n\n        gamma_ddd = 0.125 * (np.roll(d, -3, axis=0) - 3*np.roll(d, -1, axis=0) +\n                            3*np.roll(d, 1, axis=0) - np.roll(d, 3, axis=0))\n\n        numerator = np.sum(np.cross(gamma_d, gamma_dd) * gamma_ddd, axis=1)\n        denominator = vns**2 * np.abs(kappas)\n\n        torsions = numerator / denominator\n        if not signed:\n            torsions = np.abs(torsions)\n\n        if not closed:\n            torsions[:3] = torsions[3]\n            torsions[-3:] = torsions[-4]\n\n        return torsions\n\n    def close(self):\n        '''Adds the starting point to the end of the curve, so that it ends\n        exactly where it began.\n\n        '''\n        self.points = np.vstack([self.points, self.points[:1]])\n\n    def interpolate(self, num_points, s=0, **kwargs):\n        '''Replaces self.points with points from a B-spline interpolation.\n\n        This method uses scipy.interpolate.splprep. kwargs are passed\n        to this function.\n        '''\n        from scipy.interpolate import splprep, splev\n\n        lengths = self.segment_arclengths()\n        cum_length_fracs = np.cumsum(lengths) / np.sum(lengths)\n\n        tck, u = splprep([self.points[:, 0],\n                          self.points[:, 1],\n                          self.points[:, 2]],\n                         u=cum_length_fracs,\n                         **kwargs)\n        points = splev(np.linspace(0, 1, num_points),\n                            tck)\n\n        new_points = np.zeros((num_points, 3))\n        new_points[:, 0] = points[0]\n        new_points[:, 1] = points[1]\n        new_points[:, 2] = points[2]\n\n        self.points = new_points\n\n\n\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":1080,"oldLines":7,"newStart":1080,"newLines":7,"lines":["         points = self.points","         ts = np.roll(points, -1, axis=0) - k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(*angs))\n                self.k = k \n                if zero_centroid:\n                    k.zero_centroid()\n                if optimise_closure:\n                    cs = k.raw_crossings()\n                    if len(cs) > 0:\n                        closure_cs = n.argwhere(((cs[:, 0] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] < 0.)) |\n                                                ((cs[:, 1] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] > 0.)))\n                        indices = closure_cs.flatten()\n                        for index in indices:\n                            cs[index, 2:] *= -1\n                    gc = GaussCode(cs, verbose=self.verbose)\n                    gc.simplify()\n                    polys.append([angs[0], angs[1], alexander(gc, simplify=False)])\n\n                else:\n                    points = k.points\n                    closure_point = points[-1] + points[0] / 2.\n                    closure_point[2] = radius\n                    k.points = n.vstack([points, closure_point])\n\n                    polys.append([angs[0], angs[1], k.alexander_polynomial()])\n        except IndexError:\n            self.failed_case = k\n\n        self._cached_alexanders[\n            (number_of_samples, cache_radius)] = n.array(polys)\n\n        return n.array(polys)\n\n    def closure_alexander_polynomial(self, theta=0, phi=0):\n        '''Returns the Alexander polynomial of the knot, when projected in\n        the z plane after rotating the given theta and phi to the\n        North pole.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        theta : float\n            The sphere angle theta\n        phi : float\n            The sphere angle phi\n        '''\n        k = Knot(self.points, verbose=False)\n        k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(theta, phi))\n\n        cs = k.raw_crossings()\n        if len(cs) > 0:\n            closure_cs = n.argwhere(((cs[:, 0] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] < 0.)) |\n                                    ((cs[:, 1] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] > 0.)))\n            indices = closure_cs.flatten()\n            for index in indices:\n                cs[index, 2:] *= -1\n        gc = GaussCode(cs, verbose=False)\n        gc.simplify()\n        return alexander(gc, simplify=False)\n        \n\n    def alexander_fractions(self, number_of_samples=10, **kwargs):\n        '''Returns each of the Alexander polynomials from\n        self.alexander_polynomials, with the fraction of that type.\n        '''\n        polys = self.alexander_polynomials(\n            number_of_samples=number_of_samples, **kwargs)\n        alexs = n.round(polys[:, 2]).astype(n.int)\n\n        fracs = []\n        length = float(len(alexs))\n        for alex in n.unique(alexs):\n            fracs.append((alex, n.sum(alexs == alex) / length))\n\n        return sorted(fracs, key=lambda j: j[1])\n\n    def _alexander_map_values(self, number_of_samples=10, interpolation=100,\n                              **kwargs):\n        polys = self.alexander_polynomials(\n            number_of_samples=number_of_samples, **kwargs)\n\n        from scipy.interpolate import griddata\n\n          scatter_points=False,\n                           mode='imshow', interpolation=100,\n                           **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Creates (and returns) a projective diagram Returns a\n        Boolean of this information.\n\n        .. warning:: This only checks the distance by which entries in\n                     the Gauss code are separated, it is *not*\n                     guaranteed to detect virtual knots.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        virtual : bool\n            True if the Gauss code corresponds to a virtual knot. False\n            otherwise.\n\n        '''\n        gauss_code = self.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0][:, 0]\n        l = len(gauss_code)\n        total_crossings = l / 2\n        crossing_counter = 1\n        virtual = False\n\n        for crossing_number in self.gauss_code().crossing_numbers:\n            occurences = n.where(gauss_code == crossing_number)[0]\n            first_occurence = occurences[0]\n            second_occurence = occurences[1]\n            crossing_difference = second_occurence - first_occurence\n\n            if crossing_difference % 2 == 0:\n                return True\n        return False\n\n\n    def virtual_checks(self, number_of_samples=10, \n                                        zero_centroid=False):\n        '''\n        Returns a list of virtual Booleans for the curve with a given number \n        if projections taken from directions approximately evenly distributed. \n        A value of True corresponds to the projection giving a virtual knot, \n        with False returned otherwise.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        number_of_samples : int\n            The number of points on the sphere to project from.      '''\n        if zero_centroid:\n            self.zero_centroid()\n\n        angles = get_rotation_angles(number_of_samples)\n\n        polys = []\n\n        print_dist = int(max(1, 3000. / len(self.points)))\n        for i, angs in enumerate(angles):\n            if i % print_dist == 0:\n                self._vprint('\\ri = {} / {}'.format(i, len(angles)), False)\n            k = OpenKnot(self.points, verbose=False)\n            k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(*angs))\n            if zero_centroid:\n                k.zero_centroid()\n            isvirtual = k.virtual_check()\n            polys.append([angs[0], angs[1], isvirtual])\n\n        return n.array(polys)\n\n    def virtual_fractions(self, number_of_samples=10, **kwargs):\n        '''Returns each of the virtual calculates its self linking number, J(K). See Kauffman 2004 for\n        more information.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        : self_link_counter : int\n            The self linking number of the open curve\n        '''\n        k = OpenKnot(self.points, verbose=False)\n        k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(theta, phi))\n\n        # gausscode = k.gauss_code(virtual_closure=True)\n        # gausscode.simplify()\n        # gausscode = gausscode.without_virtual()._gauss_code[0]\n        gausscode = k.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0]\n        # gausscode = k.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0]\n        l = len(gausscode)\n\n        self_linking_counter = 0\n\n        cache = {}\n\n        for index, row in enumerate(gausscode):\n            number, over_under, orientation = row\n            if number in cache:\n                if ((index - cache[number]) % 2) == 0:\n                    self_linking_counter += orientation\n            else:\n                cache[number] = index\n\n        return self_linking_counter\n                    \n    def closure_alexander(self, theta=0, phi=0):\n        from pyknotid.spacecurves.knot import Knot\n        k = Knot(self.points, verbose=False)\n        k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(theta, phi))\n\n        cs = k.raw_crossings()\n        if len(cs) > 0:\n            closure_cs = n.argwhere(((cs[:, 0] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] < 0.)) |\n                                    ((cs[:, 1] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] > 0.)))\n            indices = closure_cs.flatten()\n            for index in indices:\n                cs[index, 2:] *= -1\n        gausscode = GaussCode(cs, verbose=False)\n\n        l = len(gausscode)\n\n        from pyknotid.invariants import alexander\n        return alexander(gausscode)\n                \n\n    def self_linkings(self, number_of_samples=10,\n                      zero_centroid=False, **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Returns a list of self linking numbers for the curve with a given\n        number of projections taken from directions approximately evenly\n        distributed.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        number_of_samples : int\n            The number of points on the sphere to project from. plot_alexander_shell(self, number_of_samples=100, mode='mesh',\n                             radius=None,\n                             **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Plots the curve in 3d via self.plot(), along with a translucent\n        sphere coloured by the type of knot obtained by closing on each\n        point.\n\n        Parameters are all passed to terms of the maximum Cartesian distance of any point\n        in the line from the origin.\n        '''\n\n        self.plot(**kwargs)\n\n        if radius is None:\n            self_radii =             **kwargs)\n\n\n    def alexander_polynomials_multiroots(self, number_of_samples=10,\n                                         radius=None,\n                                         zero_centroid=False):\n        '''\n        Returns a list of Alexander polynomials for the knot, closing\n        on a sphere of the given radius, with the given number of sample\n        points approximately evenly distributed on the sphere. The\n        Alexander polynomials are found at three different roots (2, 3\n        and 4) and a the knot types corresponding to these roots are\n        returned also.\n\n        The results are cached by number of samples and radius.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        number_of_samples : int\n            The number of points on the sphere to sample. Defaults to 10.\n        radius : float\n            The radius of the sphere on which to close the knot. Defaults\n            to None, which picks 10 times the largest Cartesian deviation\n            from 0.\n        zero_centroid : bool\n            Whether to first move the average position of vertices to\n            (0, 0, 0). Defaults to True.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        : ndarray\n            A number_of_samples by 3 array of angles and alexander\n            polynomials.\n        '''\n        from pyknotid.catalogue.identify import from_invariants\n        from pyknotid.catalogue.database import Knot as dbknot\n\n        if zero_centroid:\n            self.zero_centroid()\n\n        if self._cached_alexanders is not None:\n            if (number_of_samples, radius) in self._cached_alexanders:\n                return self._cached_alexanders[(number_of_samples,\n                                                radius)]\n        else:\n            self._cached_alexanders = {}\n        angles = get_rotation_angles(number_of_samples)\n\n        polys = []\n\n        cache_radius = radius\n\n        if radius is None:\n            radius = 100 * n.max(self.points)\n            # Not guaranteed to give 10* the real radius, but good enough\n\n        print_dist = int(max(1, 3000. / len(self.points)))\n        for i, angs in enumerate(angles):\n            if i % print_dist == 0:\n                self._vprint('\\ri = {} / {}'.format(i, len(angles)), False)\n            k = Knot(self.points, verbose=False)\n            k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(*angs))\n            if zero_centroid:\n                k.zero_centroid()\n            points = k.points\n            closure_point = points[-1] + points[0] / 2.\n            closure_point[2] = radius\n            k.points = n.vstack([points, closure_point])\n            root_at_two = k.alexander_at_root(2)\n            root_at_three = k.alexander_at_root(3)\n            root_at_four = k.alexander_at_root(4)\n            k_gauss_code = k.gauss_code()\n            k_gauss_code.simplify()\n            max_crossings = len(k_gauss_code)\n            if max_crossings > 17:\n                max_crossings = 18\n            knot_type = from_invariants(determinant=root_at_two, alex_imag_3=root_at_three,\n                                        alex_imag_4=root_at_four,\n                                        other=[dbknot.min_crossings <= max_crossings])\n            knot_type = knot_db_to_string(knot_type)\n            polys.append([angs[0], angs[1], root_at_two, root_at_three, root_at_four, max_crossings,\n                          knot_type])\n\n            # self._cached_alexanders[\n            #      (number_of_samples, cache_radius)] = n.array(polys)\n\n        return polys\n\n    def multiroots_fractions(self, number_of_samples=10, **kwargs):\n\n        '''\n        Returns each of the knot types from\n        self.alexander_polynomials_multiroots, with the fraction of that type.\n        '''\n\n        knot_info = self.alexander_polynomials_multiroots(\n            number_of_samples, **kwargs)\n        knot_types = []\n        for closures in knot_info:\n            knot_types.append(closures[6])\n        knot_types = [item for sublist in knot_types for item in sublist] #flattens list\n        knot_frequency = Counter(knot_types)\n        common = knot_frequency.most_common()\n        list_common = [list(elem) for elem in common]\n        list_common_fractions = [[elem[0],elem[1]/float(number_of_samples)] for elem in list_common]\n        return list_common_fractions\n\n    def generalised_alexander(self):\n        '''\n        Returns the generalised Alexander polynomial for the default projection\n        of the open knot\n        '''\n\n        gauss_code_crossings = self.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0][:, 0]\n        gauss_code_over_under = self.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0][:,1]\n        gauss_code_orientations = self.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0][:,2]\n        x = sym.var('x')\n        y = sym.var('y')\n        m_plus = sym.Matrix([[1 - x, -y], [-x * y**-1, 0]])\n        m_minus = sym.Matrix([[0, -x**-1 * y], [-y**-1, 1 - x**-1]])\n        num_crossings = len(self.gauss_code())\n        matrix = sym.zeros(2 * num_crossings, 2 *num_crossings)\n        permutation_matrix = sym.zeros(2 * num_crossings, 2 * num_crossings)\n\n        arc_labels = [0]*len(gauss_code_crossings)\n        for i in range(len(gauss_code_crossings)):\n            arc_labels[i] = [0] * 4\n\n        counter = 0\n        for crossing_number in self.gauss_code().crossing_numbers:\n            occurrences = n.where(gauss_code_crossings == crossing_number)[0]\n            if gauss_code_orientations[occurrences[0]] == 1:\n                m = m_plus\n            else:\n                m = m_minus\n            for i in [0,1]:\n                for j in [0,1]:\n                    matrix[counter*2 + i,counter*2 + j] = m[i,j]\n            counter += 1\n\n        for i in range(len(gauss_code_crossings)):\n Defaults to 10.\n        optimise_closure: bool\n            If True, doesn't really close on a sphere but at infinity.\n            This lets the calculation be optimised slightly, and so is the\n            default.\n        radius : float\n            The radius of the sphere on which to close the knot. Defaults\n            to None, which picks 10 times the largest Cartesian deviation\n            from 0. This is *only* used if optimise_closure=False.\n        zero_centroid : bool\n            Whether to first move the average position of vertices to\n            (0, 0, 0). Defaults to True.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        : ndarray\n            A number_of_samples by 3 array of angles and alexander\n            polynomials.\n        '''\n        if zero_centroid:\n            self.zero_centroid()\n\n        if not recalculate and self._cached_alexanders is not None:\n            if (number_of_samples, radius) in self._cached_alexanders:\n                return self._cached_alexanders[(number_of_samples,\n                                                radius)]\n        else:\n            self._cached_alexanders = {}\n        angles = get_rotation_angles(number_of_samples)\n\n        polys = []\n\n        cache_radius = radius\n\n        if radius is None:\n            radius = 100 * n.max(self.points)\n            # Not guaranteed to give 10* the real radius, but good enough\n\n        print_dist = int(max(1, 3000. / len(self.points)))\n        try:\n            for i, angs in enumerate(angles):\n                if i % print_dist == 0:\n                    self._vprint('\\ri = {} / {}'.format(i, len(angles)), False)\n                k = Knot(self.points, verbose=False)\n                k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(*angs))\n                self.k = k \n                if zero_centroid:\n                    k.zero_centroid()\n                if optimise_closure:\n                    cs = k.raw_crossings()\n                    if len(cs) > 0:\n                        closure_cs = n.argwhere(((cs[:, 0] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] < 0.)) |\n                                                ((cs[:, 1] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] > 0.)))\n                        indices = closure_cs.flatten()\n                        for index in indices:\n                            cs[index, 2:] *= -1\n                    gc = GaussCode(cs, verbose=self.verbose)\n                    gc.simplify()\n                    polys.append([angs[0], angs[1], alexander(gc, simplify=False)])\n\n                else:\n                    points = k.points\n                    closure_point = points[-1] + points[0] / 2.\n                    closure_point[2] = radius\n                    k.points = n.vstack([points, closure_point])\n\n                    polys.append([angs[0], angs[1], k.alexander_polynomial()])\n        except IndexError:\n            self.failed_case = k\n\n        self._cached_alexanders[\n            (number_of_samples, cache_radius)] = n.array(polys)\n\n        return n.array(polys)\n\n    def closure_alexander_polynomial(self, theta=0, phi=0):\n        '''Returns the Alexander polynomial of the knot, when projected in\n        the z plane after rotating the given theta and phi to the\n        North pole.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        theta : float\n            The sphere angle theta\n        phi : float\n            The sphere angle phi\n        '''\n        k = Knot(self.points, verbose=False)\n        k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(theta, phi))\n\n        cs = k.raw_crossings()\n        if len(cs) > 0:\n            closure_cs = n.argwhere(((cs[:, 0] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] < 0.)) |\n                                    ((cs[:, 1] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] > 0.)))\n            indices = closure_cs.flatten()\n            for index in indices:\n                cs[index, 2:] *= -1\n        gc = GaussCode(cs, verbose=False)\n        gc.simplify()\n        return alexander(gc, simplify=False)\n        \n\n    def alexander_fractions(self, number_of_samples=10, **kwargs):\n        '''Returns each of the Alexander polynomials from\n        self.alexander_polynomials, with the fraction of that type.\n        '''\n        polys = self.alexander_polynomials(\n            number_of_samples=number_of_samples, **kwargs)\n        alexs = n.round(polys[:, 2]).astype(n.int64)\n\n        fracs = []\n        length = float(len(alexs))\n        for alex in n.unique(alexs):\n            fracs.append((alex, n.sum(alexs == alex) / length))\n\n        return sorted(fracs, key=lambda j: j[1])\n\n    def _alexander_map_values(self, number_of_samples=10, interpolation=100,\n                              **kwargs):\n        polys = self.alexander_polynomials(\n            number_of_samples=number_of_samples, **kwargs)\n\n        from scipy.interpolate import griddata\n\n        positions = []\n        for i, row in enumerate(polys):\n            positions.append(gall_peters(row[0], row[1]))\n        positions = n.array(positions)\n\n        interpolation_points = n.mgrid[0:2*n.pi:int(1.57*interpolation)*1j,\n                                       -2.:2.:interpolation*1j]\n        # interpolation_points = n.mgrid[0:2 * n.pi:157j,\n        #                        -2.:2.:100j]\n        # '''interpolation_points = n.mgrid[-n.pi:n.pi:157j,\n        #                        -n.pi/2:n.pi/2:100j]'''\n\n        values = griddata(positions, polys[:, 2],\n                          tuple(interpolation_points),\n                          method='nearest')\n\n        return positions, values\n\n    def plot_alexander_map(self, number_of_samples=10,\n                           scatter_points=False,\n                           mode='imshow', interpolation=100,\n                           **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Creates (and returns) a projective diagram showing each\n        different Alexander polynomial in a different colour according\n        to a closure on a far away point in this direction.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        number_of_samples : int\n            The number of points on the sphere to close at.\n        scatter_points : bool\n            If True, plots a dot at each point on the map projection\n            where a closure was made.\n        mode : str\n            'imshow' to plot the pixels of an image, otherwise plots\n            filled contours. Defaults to 'imshow'.\n        interpolation : int\n            The (short) side length of the interpolation grid on which\n            the map projection is made. Defaults to 100.\n        '''\n\n        positions, values = self._alexander_map_values(\n            number_of_samples,\n            interpolation=interpolation)\n\n        import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\n        fig, ax = plt.subplots()\n        '''fig = plt.figure(figsize=(10, 5))\n        ax = fig.add_subplot(111, projection=\"mollweide\")\n        ax.grid(True)'''\n\n        if mode == 'imshow':\n            cax = ax.imshow(values.T, cmap='jet', interpolation='none')\n            fig.colorbar(cax)\n        else:\n            ax.contourf(values.T, cmap='jet',\n                        levels=[0] + range(3, int(n.max(values) + 1.1), 2))\n        ax.set_xticks([])\n        ax.set_yticks([])\n\n        ax.set_xlim(-0.5, 1.565*interpolation)\n        ax.set_ylim(-0.5, 0.995*interpolation)\n\n        im_positions = positions*25*float(interpolation / 100.)\n        im_positions[:, 0] -= 0.5\n        im_positions[:, 1] += 49.*(interpolation / 100.) + 0.5\n        if scatter_points:\n            ax.scatter(im_positions[:, 0], im_positions[:, 1], color='black',\n                       alpha=1, s=1)\n\n        fig.tight_layout()\n        fig.show()\n        return fig, ax\n\n\n\n    def virtual_check(self):\n        '''Takes an open curve and checks (for the default projection) if its\n        Gauss code corresponds to a virtual knot or not. Returns a\n        Boolean of this information.\n\n        .. warning:: This only checks the distance by which entries in\n                     the Gauss code are separated, it is *not*\n                     guaranteed to detect virtual knots.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        virtual : bool\n            True if the Gauss code corresponds to a virtual knot. False\n            otherwise.\n\n        '''\n        gauss_code = self.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0][:, 0]\n        l = len(gauss_code)\n        total_crossings = l / 2\n        crossing_counter = 1\n        virtual = False\n\n        for crossing_number in self.gauss_code().crossing_numbers:\n            occurences = n.where(gauss_code == crossing_number)[0]\n            first_occurence = occurences[0]\n            second_occurence = occurences[1]\n            crossing_difference = second_occurence - first_occurence\n\n            if crossing_difference % 2 == 0:\n                return True\n        return False\n\n\n    def virtual_checks(self, number_of_samples=10, \n                                        zero_centroid=False):\n        '''\n        Returns a list of virtual Booleans for the curve with a given number \n        if projections taken from directions approximately evenly distributed. \n        A value of True corresponds to the projection giving a virtual knot, \n        with False returned otherwise.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        number_of_samples : int\n            The number of points on the sphere to project from. Defaults to 10.\n        zero_centroid : bool\n            Whether to first move the average position of vertices to\n            (0, 0, 0). Defaults to False.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        : ndarray\n            A number_of_samples by 3 array of angles and virtual Booleans\n            (True if virtual, False otherwise)\n        '''\n        if zero_centroid:\n            self.zero_centroid()\n\n        angles = get_rotation_angles(number_of_samples)\n\n        polys = []\n\n        print_dist = int(max(1, 3000. / len(self.points)))\n        for i, angs in enumerate(angles):\n            if i % print_dist == 0:\n                self._vprint('\\ri = {} / {}'.format(i, len(angles)), False)\n            k = OpenKnot(self.points, verbose=False)\n            k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(*angs))\n            if zero_centroid:\n                k.zero_centroid()\n            isvirtual = k.virtual_check()\n            polys.append([angs[0], angs[1], isvirtual])\n\n        return n.array(polys)\n\n    def virtual_fractions(self, number_of_samples=10, **kwargs):\n        '''Returns each of the virtual / length))\n\n        return sorted(fracs, key=lambda j: j[1])\n        \n    def _virtual_map_values(self, number_of_samples=10, **kwargs):\n        polys = self.virtual_checks(\n fig.tight_layout()\n        fig.show()\n\n        return fig, ax\n\n    def plot_virtual_shell(self, number_of_samples=10,\n                           zero_centroid=False,\n                           sphere_radius_factor=2.,\n                           opacity=0.3, **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Plots the curve in 3d via self.plot(), along with a translucent\n        sphere coloured according to       zs = r * n.cos(thetas)\n        xs = r * n.sin(thetas) * n.cos(phis)\n        ys = r * n.sin(thetas) * n.sin(phis)\n\n        import mayavi.mlab as may\n\n        may.mesh(xs, ys, zs, scalars=values, opacity=opacity, **kwargs)\n\n    def self_linking(self, theta=0, phi=0):\n        '''\n        Takes an open curve, finds its Gauss code (for the default projection)\n        and calculates its self linking number, J(K). See Kauffman 2004 for\n        more information.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        : self_link_counter : int\n            The self linking number of the open curve\n        '''\n        k = OpenKnot(self.points, verbose=False)\n        k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(theta, phi))\n\n        # gausscode = k.gauss_code(virtual_closure=True)\n        # gausscode.simplify()\n        # gausscode = gausscode.without_virtual()._gauss_code[0]\n        gausscode = k.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0]\n        # gausscode = k.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0]\n        l = len(gausscode)\n\n        self_linking_counter = 0\n\n        cache = {}\n\n        for index, row in enumerate(gausscode):\n            number, over_under, orientation = row\n            if number in cache:\n                if ((index - cache[number]) % 2) == 0:\n                    self_linking_counter += orientation\n            else:\n                cache[number] = index\n\n        return self_linking_counter\n                    \n    def closure_alexander(self, theta=0, phi=0):\n        from pyknotid.spacecurves.knot import Knot\n        k = Knot(self.points, verbose=False)\n        k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(theta, phi))\n\n        cs = k.raw_crossings()\n        if len(cs) > 0:\n            closure_cs = n.argwhere(((cs[:, 0] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] < 0.)) |\n                                    ((cs[:, 1] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] > 0.)))\n            indices = closure_cs.flatten()\n            for index in indices:\n                cs[index, 2:] *= -1\n        gausscode = GaussCode(cs, verbose=False)\n\n        l = len(gausscode)\n\n        from pyknotid.invariants import alexander\n        return alexander(gausscode)\n                \n\n    def self_linkings(self, number_of_samples=10,\n                      zero_centroid=False, **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Returns a list of self linking numbers for the curve with a given\n        number of projections taken from directions approximately evenly\n        distributed.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        number_of_samples : int\n            The number of points on the sphere to project from.     self.zero_centroid()\n\n        angles = get_rotation_angles(number_of_samples)\n\n        polys = []\n\n        print_dist = int(max(1, 3000. / len(self.points)))\n        for i, angs in k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(*angs))\n            if zero_centroid:\n                k.zero_centroid()\n            self_linking = k.self_linking()\n            polys.append([angs[0], angs[1], self_linking])\n\n        return n.array(polys)\n\n    def self_linking_fractions(self, number_of_samples=10, **kwargs):\n        '''Returns each of the self linking numbers from\n  \n    def _self_linking_map_values(self, number_of_samples=10, **kwargs):\n        polys = self.self_linkings(\n            number_of_samples=number_of_samples, **kwargs)\n\n        from plot_alexander_shell(self, number_of_samples=100, mode='mesh',\n                             radius=None,\n                             **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Plots the curve in 3d via self.plot(), along with a translucent\n        sphere coloured by the type of knot obtained by closing on each\n        point.\n\n        Parameters are all passed to :meth:`OpenKnot.alexander_polynomials`,\n        except opacity and kwargs which are given to mayavi.mesh, and\n        sphere_radius_factor which gives the radius of the enclosing\n        sphere in terms of the maximum Cartesian distance of any point\n        in the line from the origin.\n        '''\n\n        self.plot(**kwargs)\n\n        if radius is None:\n            self_radii = n.sqrt(n.sum(self.points*self.points, axis=1))\n            radius = n.max(self_radii) * 2\n        print('radius is', radius)\n        if mode == 'mesh':\n            plot_sphere_shell_vispy(self.closure_alexander_polynomial,\n                                    number_of_samples, number_of_samples,\n                                    radius=radius,\n                                    **kwargs)\n        elif mode == 'crude':\n            plot_shell(self._alexander_map_values, self.points,\n                       number_of_samples=number_of_samples,\n                       **kwargs)\n\n\n    def alexander_polynomials_multiroots(self, number_of_samples=10,\n                                         radius=None,\n                                         zero_centroid=False):\n        '''\n        Returns a list of Alexander polynomials for the knot, closing\n        on a sphere of the given radius, with the given number of sample\n        points approximately evenly distributed on the sphere. The\n        Alexander polynomials are found at three different roots (2, 3\n        and 4) and a the knot types corresponding to these roots are\n        returned also.\n\n        The results are cached by number of samples and radius.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        number_of_samples : int\n            The number of points on the sphere to sample. Defaults to 10.\n        radius : float\n            The radius of the sphere on which to close the knot. Defaults\n            to None, which picks 10 times the largest Cartesian deviation\n            from 0.\n        zero_centroid : bool\n            Whether to first move the average position of vertices to\n            (0, 0, 0). Defaults to True.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        : ndarray\n            A number_of_samples by 3 array of angles and alexander\n            polynomials.\n        '''\n        from pyknotid.catalogue.identify import from_invariants\n        from pyknotid.catalogue.database import Knot as dbknot\n\n        if zero_centroid:\n            self.zero_centroid()\n\n        if self._cached_alexanders is not None:\n            if (number_of_samples, radius) in self._cached_alexanders:\n                return self._cached_alexanders[(number_of_samples,\n                                                radius)]\n        else:\n            self._cached_alexanders = {}\n        angles = get_rotation_angles(number_of_samples)\n\n        polys = []\n\n        cache_radius = radius\n\n        if radius is None:\n            radius = 100 * n.max(self.points)\n            # Not guaranteed to give 10* the real radius, but good enough\n\n        print_dist = int(max(1, 3000. / len(self.points)))\n        for i, angs in enumerate(angles):\n            if i % print_dist == 0:\n                self._vprint('\\ri = {} / {}'.format(i, len(angles)), False)\n            k = Knot(self.points, verbose=False)\n            k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(*angs))\n            if zero_centroid:\n                k.zero_centroid()\n            points = k.points\n            closure_point = points[-1] + points[0] / 2.\n            closure_point[2] = radius\n            k.points = n.vstack([points, closure_point])\n            root_at_two = k.alexander_at_root(2)\n            root_at_three = k.alexander_at_root(3)\n            root_at_four = k.alexander_at_root(4)\n            k_gauss_code = k.gauss_code()\n            k_gauss_code.simplify()\n            max_crossings = len(k_gauss_code)\n            if max_crossings > 17:\n                max_crossings = 18\n            knot_type = from_invariants(determinant=root_at_two, alex_imag_3=root_at_three,\n                                        alex_imag_4=root_at_four,\n                                        other=[dbknot.min_crossings <= max_crossings])\n            knot_type = knot_db_to_string(knot_type)\n            polys.append([angs[0], angs[1], root_at_two, root_at_three, root_at_four, max_crossings,\n                          knot_type])\n\n            # self._cached_alexanders[\n            #      (number_of_samples, cache_radius)] = n.array(polys)\n\n        return polys\n\n    def multiroots_fractions(self, number_of_samples=10, **kwargs):\n\n        '''\n        Returns each of the knot types from\n        self.alexander_polynomials_multiroots, with the fraction of that type.\n        '''\n\n        knot_info = self.alexander_polynomials_multiroots(\n            number_of_samples, **kwargs)\n        knot_types = []\n        for closures in knot_info:\n            knot_types.append(closures[6])\n        knot_types = [item for sublist in knot_types for item in sublist] #flattens list\n        knot_frequency = Counter(knot_types)\n        common = knot_frequency.most_common()\n        list_common = [list(elem) for elem in common]\n        list_common_fractions = [[elem[0],elem[1]/float(number_of_samples)] for elem in list_common]\n        return list_common_fractions\n\n    def generalised_alexander(self):\n        '''\n        Returns the generalised Alexander polynomial for the default projection\n        of the open knot\n        '''\n\n        gauss_code_crossings = self.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0][:, 0]\n        gauss_code_over_under = self.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0][:,1]\n        gauss_code_orientations = self.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0][:,2]\n        x = sym.var('x')\n        y = sym.var('y')\n        m_plus = sym.Matrix([[1 - x, -y], [-x * y**-1, 0]])\n        m_minus = sym.Matrix([[0, -x**-1 * y], [-y**-1, 1 - x**-1]])\n        num_crossings = len(self.gauss_code())\n        matrix = sym.zeros(2 * num_crossings, 2 *num_crossings)\n        permutation_matrix = sym.zeros(2 * num_crossings, 2 * num_crossings)\n\n        arc_labels = [0]*len(gauss_code_crossings)\n        for i in range(len(gauss_code_crossings)):\n            arc_labels[i] = [0] * 4\n\n        counter = 0\n        for crossing_number in self.gauss_code().crossing_numbers:\n            occurrences = n.where(gauss_code_crossings == crossing_number)[0]\n            if gauss_code_orientations[occurrences[0]] == 1:\n                m = m_plus\n            else:\n                m = m_minus\n            for i in [0,1]:\n                for j in [0,1]:\n                    matrix[counter*2 + i,counter*2 + j] = m[i,j]\n            counter += 1\n\n        for i in range(len(gauss_code_crossings)):\n            arc_labels[i][0] = gauss_code_crossings[i]\n            arc_labels[i][1] = (gauss_code_orientations[i] *\n                                gauss_code_over_under[i]) #-1 = r, +1 = l\n            arc_labels[i-1][2] = gauss_code_crossings[i]\n            arc_labels[i-1][3] = (-1 * gauss_code_orientations[i] *\n                                  gauss_code_over_under[i]) #-1 = r, +1 = l\n\n        counter = 1\n        for crossing_number in self.gauss_code().crossing_numbers:\n            for i in range(len(gauss_code_crossings)):\n                if arc_labels[i][0] == crossing_number:\n                    arc_labels[i][0] = counter\n                if arc_labels[i][2] == crossing_number:\n                    arc_labels[i][2] = counter\n            counter += 1\n\n        for i in range(len(arc_labels)):\n            if arc_labels[i][1] < 0:\n                if arc_labels[i][3] < 0 :\n                    #bottom right\n                    permutation_matrix[arc_labels[i][2]*2-1, arc_labels[i][0]*2-1] = 1\n                else:\n                    #bottom left\n                    permutation_matrix[arc_labels[i][2]*2-2, arc_labels[i][0]*2-1] = 1\n            else:\n                if arc_labels[i][3] < 0:\n                    #upper right\n                    permutation_matrix[arc_labels[i][2]*2-1, arc_labels[i][0]*2-2] = 1\n                else:\n                    #upper left\n                    permutation_matrix[arc_labels[i][2]*2-2, arc_labels[i][0]*2-2] = 1\n\n        writhe = sum(gauss_code_orientations)/2\n\n        return (-1)**writhe * ((matrix - permutation_matrix).det())\n\n    def projection_invariant(self, **kwargs):\n        '''\n        First checks if the
