the given radius, with the given number of sample\n        points approximately evenly distributed on the sphere.\n\n        The results are cached by number of samples and radius.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        number_of_samples : int\n            The number of points on the sphere to sample. Defaults to 10.\n        optimise_closure: bool\n            If True, doesn't really close on a sphere but at infinity.\n            This lets the calculation be optimised slightly, and so is the\n            default.\n        radius : float\n            The radius of the sphere on which to close the knot. Defaults\n            to None, which picks 10 times the largest Cartesian deviation\n            from 0. This is *only* used if optimise_closure=False.\n        zero_centroid : bool\n            Whether to first move the average position of vertices to\n            (0, 0, 0). Defaults to True.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        : ndarray\n            A number_of_samples by 3 array of angles and alexander\n            polynomials.\n        '''\n        if zero_centroid:\n            self.zero_centroid()\n\n        if not recalculate and self._cached_alexanders is not None:\n            if (number_of_samples, radius) in self._cached_alexanders:\n                return self._cached_alexanders[(number_of_samples,\n                                                radius)]\n        else:\n            self._cached_alexanders = {}\n        angles = get_rotation_angles(number_of_samples)\n\n        polys = []\n\n        cache_radius = radius\n\n        if radius is None:\n            radius = 100 * n.max(self.points)\n            # Not guaranteed to give 10* the real radius, but good enough\n\n        print_dist = int(max(1, 3000. / len(self.points)))\n        try:\n            for i, angs in enumerate(angles):\n                if i % print_dist == 0:\n                    self._vprint('\\ri = {} / {}'.format(i, len(angles)), False)\n                k = Knot(self.points, verbose=False)\n                k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(*angs))\n                self.k = k \n                if zero_centroid:\n                    k.zero_centroid()\n                if optimise_closure:\n                    cs = k.raw_crossings()\n                    if len(cs) > 0:\n                        closure_cs = n.argwhere(((cs[:, 0] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] < 0.)) |\n                                                ((cs[:, 1] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] > 0.)))\n                        indices = closure_cs.flatten()\n                        for index in indices:\n                            cs[index, 2:] *= -1\n                    gc = GaussCode(cs, verbose=self.verbose)\n                    gc.simplify()\n                    polys.append([angs[0], angs[1], alexander(gc, simplify=False)])\n\n                else:\n                    points = k.points\n                    closure_point = points[-1] + points[0] / 2.\n                    closure_point[2] = radius\n                    k.points = n.vstack([points, closure_point])\n\n                    polys.append([angs[0], angs[1], k.alexander_polynomial()])\n        except IndexError:\n            self.failed_case = k\n\n        self._cached_alexanders[\n            (number_of_samples, cache_radius)] = n.array(polys)\n\n        return n.array(polys)\n\n    def closure_alexander_polynomial(self, theta=0, phi=0):\n        '''Returns the Alexander polynomial of the knot, when projected in\n        the z plane after rotating the given theta and phi to the\n        North pole.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        theta : float\n            The sphere angle theta\n        phi : float\n            The sphere angle phi\n        '''\n        k = Knot(self.points, verbose=False)\n        k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(theta, phi))\n\n        cs = k.raw_crossings()\n        if len(cs) > 0:\n            closure_cs = n.argwhere(((cs[:, 0] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] < 0.)) |\n                                    ((cs[:, 1] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] > 0.)))\n            indices = closure_cs.flatten()\n            for index in indices:\n                cs[index, 2:] *= -1\n        gc = GaussCode(cs, verbose=False)\n        gc.simplify()\n        return alexander(gc, simplify=False)\n        \n\n    def alexander_fractions(self, number_of_samples=10, **kwargs):\n        '''Returns each of the Alexander polynomials from\n        self.alexander_polynomials, with the fraction of that type.\n        '''\n        polys = self.alexander_polynomials(\n            number_of_samples=number_of_samples, **kwargs)\n        alexs = n.round(polys[:, 2]).astype(n.int64)\n\n        fracs = []\n        length = float(len(alexs))\n        for alex in n.unique(alexs):\n            fracs.append((alex, n.sum(alexs == alex) / length))\n\n        return sorted(fracs, key=lambda j: j[1])\n\n    def _alexander_map_values(self, number_of_samples=10, interpolation=100,\n                              **kwargs):\n        polys = self.alexander_polynomials(\n            number_of_samples=number_of_samples, **kwargs)\n\n        from scipy.interpolate import griddata\n\n        positions = []\n        for i, row in enumerate(polys):\n            positions.append(gall_peters(row[0], row[1]))\n        positions = n.array(positions)\n\n        interpolation_points = n.mgrid[0:2*n.pi:int(1.57*interpolation)*1j,\n                                       -2.:2.:interpolation*1j]\n        # interpolation_points = n.mgrid[0:2 * n.pi:157j,\n        #                        -2.:2.:100j]\n        # '''interpolation_points = n.mgrid[-n.pi:n.pi:157j,\n        #                        -n.pi/2:n.pi/2:100j]'''\n\n        values = griddata(positions, polys[:, 2],\n                          tuple(interpolation_points),\n                          method='nearest')\n\n        return positions, values\n\n    def plot_alexander_map(self, number_of_samples=10,\n                           scatter_points=False,\n                           mode='imshow', interpolation=100,\n                           **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Creates (and returns) a projective diagram showing each\n        different Alexander polynomial in a different colour according\n        to a closure on a far away point in this direction.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        number_of_samples : int\n            The number of points on the sphere to close at.\n        scatter_points : bool\n            If True, plots a dot at each point on the map projection\n            where a closure was made.\n        mode : str\n            'imshow' to plot the pixels of an image, otherwise plots\n            filled contours. Defaults to 'imshow'.\n        interpolation : int\n            The (short) side length of the interpolation grid on which\n            the map projection is made. Defaults to 100.\n        '''\n\n        positions, values = self._alexander_map_values(\n            number_of_samples,\n            interpolation=interpolation)\n\n        import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n\n        fig, ax = plt.subplots()\n        '''fig = plt.figure(figsize=(10, 5))\n        ax = fig.add_subplot(111, projection=\"mollweide\")\n        ax.grid(True)'''\n\n        if mode == 'imshow':\n            cax = ax.imshow(values.T, cmap='jet', interpolation='none')\n            fig.colorbar(cax)\n        else:\n            ax.contourf(values.T, cmap='jet',\n                        levels=[0] + range(3, int(n.max(values) + 1.1), 2))\n        ax.set_xticks([])\n        ax.set_yticks([])\n\n        ax.set_xlim(-0.5, 1.565*interpolation)\n        ax.set_ylim(-0.5, 0.995*interpolation)\n\n        im_positions = positions*25*float(interpolation / 100.)\n        im_positions[:, 0] -= 0.5\n        im_positions[:, 1] += 49.*(interpolation / 100.) + 0.5\n        if scatter_points:\n            ax.scatter(im_positions[:, 0], im_positions[:, 1], color='black',\n                       alpha=1, s=1)\n\n        fig.tight_layout()\n        fig.show()\n        return fig, ax\n\n\n\n    def virtual_check(self):\n        '''Takes an open curve and checks (for the default projection) if its\n        Gauss code corresponds to a virtual knot or not. Returns a\n        Boolean of this information.\n\n        .. warning:: This only checks the distance by which entries in\n                     the Gauss code are separated, it is *not*\n                     guaranteed to detect virtual knots.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        virtual : bool\n            True if the Gauss code corresponds to a virtual knot. False\n            otherwise.\n\n        '''\n        gauss_code = self.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0][:, 0]\n        l = len(gauss_code)\n        total_crossings = l / 2\n        crossing_counter = 1\n        virtual = False\n\n        for crossing_number in self.gauss_code().crossing_numbers:\n            occurences = n.where(gauss_code == crossing_number)[0]\n            first_occurence = occurences[0]\n            second_occurence = occurences[1]\n            crossing_difference = second_occurence - first_occurence\n\n            if crossing_difference % 2 == 0:\n                return True\n        return False\n\n\n    def virtual_checks(self, number_of_samples=10, \n                                        zero_centroid=False):\n        '''\n        Returns a list of virtual Booleans for the curve with a given number \n        if projections taken from directions approximately evenly distributed. \n        A value of True corresponds to the projection giving a virtual knot, \n        with False returned otherwise.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        number_of_samples : int\n            The number of points on the sphere to project from. Defaults to 10.\n        zero_centroid : bool\n            Whether to first move the average position of vertices to\n            (0, 0, 0). Defaults to False.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        : ndarray\n            A number_of_samples by 3 array of angles and virtual Booleans\n            (True if virtual, False otherwise)\n        '''\n        if zero_centroid:\n            self.zero_centroid()\n\n        angles = get_rotation_angles(number_of_samples)\n\n        polys = []\n\n        print_dist = int(max(1, 3000. / len(self.points)))\n        for i, angs in enumerate(angles):\n            if i % print_dist == 0:\n                self._vprint('\\ri = {} / {}'.format(i, len(angles)), False)\n            k = OpenKnot(self.points, verbose=False)\n            k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(*angs))\n            if zero_centroid:\n                k.zero_centroid()\n            isvirtual = k.virtual_check()\n            polys.append([angs[0], angs[1], isvirtual])\n\n        return n.array(polys)\n\n    def virtual_fractions(self, number_of_samples=10, **kwargs):\n        '''Returns each of the virtual       alexs = n.round(polys[:, 2]).astype(n.int64)\n\n        fracs = []\n        length = float(len(alexs))\n        for alex in n.unique(alexs):\n            fracs.append((alex, n.sum(alexs == alex) / length))\n\n        return sorted(fracs, key=lambda j: j[1])\n        \n    def _virtual_map_values(self, number_of_samples=10, **kwargs):\n        polys = self.virtual_checks(\n fig.tight_layout()\n        fig.show()\n\n        return fig, ax\n\n    def plot_virtual_shell(self, number_of_samples=10,\n                           zero_centroid=False,\n                           sphere_radius_factor=2.,\n                           opacity=0.3, **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Plots the curve in 3d via self.plot(), along with a translucent\n        sphere coloured according to       zs = r * n.cos(thetas)\n        xs = r * n.sin(thetas) * n.cos(phis)\n        ys = r * n.sin(thetas) * n.sin(phis)\n\n        import mayavi.mlab as may\n\n        may.mesh(xs, ys, zs, scalars=values, opacity=opacity, **kwargs)\n\n    def self_linking(self, theta=0, phi=0):\n        '''\n        Takes an open curve, finds its Gauss code (for the default projection)\n        and calculates its self linking number, J(K). See Kauffman 2004 for\n        more information.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        : self_link_counter : int\n            The self linking number of the open curve\n        '''\n        k = OpenKnot(self.points, verbose=False)\n        k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(theta, phi))\n\n        # gausscode = k.gauss_code(virtual_closure=True)\n        # gausscode.simplify()\n        # gausscode = gausscode.without_virtual()._gauss_code[0]\n        gausscode = k.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0]\n        # gausscode = k.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0]\n        l = len(gausscode)\n\n        self_linking_counter = 0\n\n        cache = {}\n\n        for index, row in enumerate(gausscode):\n            number, over_under, orientation = row\n            if number in cache:\n                if ((index - cache[number]) % 2) == 0:\n                    self_linking_counter += orientation\n            else:\n                cache[number] = index\n\n        return self_linking_counter\n                    \n    def closure_alexander(self, theta=0, phi=0):\n        from pyknotid.spacecurves.knot import Knot\n        k = Knot(self.points, verbose=False)\n        k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(theta, phi))\n\n        cs = k.raw_crossings()\n        if len(cs) > 0:\n            closure_cs = n.argwhere(((cs[:, 0] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] < 0.)) |\n                                    ((cs[:, 1] > len(self.points)-1) & (cs[:, 2] > 0.)))\n            indices = closure_cs.flatten()\n            for index in indices:\n                cs[index, 2:] *= -1\n        gausscode = GaussCode(cs, verbose=False)\n\n        l = len(gausscode)\n\n        from pyknotid.invariants import alexander\n        return alexander(gausscode)\n                \n\n    def self_linkings(self, number_of_samples=10,\n                      zero_centroid=False, **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Returns a list of self linking numbers for the curve with a given\n        number of projections taken from directions approximately evenly\n        distributed.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        number_of_samples : int\n            The number of points on the sphere to project from.     self.zero_centroid()\n\n        angles = get_rotation_angles(number_of_samples)\n\n        polys = []\n\n        print_dist = int(max(1, 3000. / len(self.points)))\n        for i, angs in k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(*angs))\n            if zero_centroid:\n                k.zero_centroid()\n            self_linking = k.self_linking()\n            polys.append([angs[0], angs[1], self_linking])\n\n        return n.array(polys)\n\n    def self_linking_fractions(self, number_of_samples=10, **kwargs):\n        '''Returns each of the self linking numbers from\n  \n    def _self_linking_map_values(self, number_of_samples=10, **kwargs):\n        polys = self.self_linkings(\n            number_of_samples=number_of_samples, **kwargs)\n\n        from plot_alexander_shell(self, number_of_samples=100, mode='mesh',\n                             radius=None,\n                             **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Plots the curve in 3d via self.plot(), along with a translucent\n        sphere coloured by the type of knot obtained by closing on each\n        point.\n\n        Parameters are all passed to :meth:`OpenKnot.alexander_polynomials`,\n        except opacity and kwargs which are given to mayavi.mesh, and\n        sphere_radius_factor which gives the radius of the enclosing\n        sphere in terms of the maximum Cartesian distance of any point\n        in the line from the origin.\n        '''\n\n        self.plot(**kwargs)\n\n        if radius is None:\n            self_radii = n.sqrt(n.sum(self.points*self.points, axis=1))\n            radius = n.max(self_radii) * 2\n        print('radius is', radius)\n        if mode == 'mesh':\n                       **kwargs)\n        elif mode == 'crude':\n            plot_shell(self._alexander_map_values, self.points,\n                       number_of_samples=number_of_samples,\n                       **kwargs)\n\n\n    def alexander_polynomials_multiroots(self, number_of_samples=10,\n                                         radius=None,\n                                         zero_centroid=False):\n        '''\n        Returns a list of Alexander polynomials for the knot, closing\n        on a sphere of the given radius, with the given number of sample\n        points approximately evenly distributed on the sphere. The\n        Alexander polynomials are found at three different roots (2, 3\n        and 4) and a the knot types corresponding to these roots are\n        returned also.\n\n        The results are cached by number of samples and radius.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        number_of_samples : int\n            The number of points on the sphere to sample. Defaults to 10.\n        radius : float\n            The radius of the sphere on which to close the knot. Defaults\n            to None, which picks 10 times the largest Cartesian deviation\n            from 0.\n        zero_centroid : bool\n            Whether to first move the average position of vertices to\n            (0, 0, 0). Defaults to True.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        : ndarray\n            A number_of_samples by 3 array of angles and alexander\n            polynomials.\n        '''\n        from pyknotid.catalogue.identify import from_invariants\n        from pyknotid.catalogue.database import Knot as dbknot\n\n        if zero_centroid:\n            self.zero_centroid()\n\n        if self._cached_alexanders is not None:\n            if (number_of_samples, radius) in self._cached_alexanders:\n                return self._cached_alexanders[(number_of_samples,\n                                                radius)]\n        else:\n            self._cached_alexanders = {}\n        angles = get_rotation_angles(number_of_samples)\n\n        polys = []\n\n        cache_radius = radius\n\n        if radius is None:\n            radius = 100 * n.max(self.points)\n            # Not guaranteed to give 10* the real radius, but good enough\n\n        print_dist = int(max(1, 3000. / len(self.points)))\n        for i, angs in enumerate(angles):\n            if i % print_dist == 0:\n                self._vprint('\\ri = {} / {}'.format(i, len(angles)), False)\n            k = Knot(self.points, verbose=False)\n            k._apply_matrix(rotate_to_top(*angs))\n            if zero_centroid:\n                k.zero_centroid()\n            points = k.points\n            closure_point = points[-1] + points[0] / 2.\n            closure_point[2] = radius\n            k.points = n.vstack([points, closure_point])\n            root_at_two = k.alexander_at_root(2)\n            root_at_three = k.alexander_at_root(3)\n            root_at_four = k.alexander_at_root(4)\n            k_gauss_code = k.gauss_code()\n            k_gauss_code.simplify()\n            max_crossings = len(k_gauss_code)\n            if max_crossings > 17:\n                max_crossings = 18\n            knot_type = from_invariants(determinant=root_at_two, alex_imag_3=root_at_three,\n                                        alex_imag_4=root_at_four,\n                                        other=[dbknot.min_crossings <= max_crossings])\n            knot_type = knot_db_to_string(knot_type)\n            polys.append([angs[0], angs[1], root_at_two, root_at_three, root_at_four, max_crossings,\n                          knot_type])\n\n            # self._cached_alexanders[\n            #      (number_of_samples, cache_radius)] = n.array(polys)\n\n        return polys\n\n    def multiroots_fractions(self, number_of_samples=10, **kwargs):\n\n        '''\n        Returns each of the knot types from\n        self.alexander_polynomials_multiroots, with the fraction of that type.\n        '''\n\n        knot_info = self.alexander_polynomials_multiroots(\n            number_of_samples, **kwargs)\n        knot_types = []\n        for closures in knot_info:\n            knot_types.append(closures[6])\n        knot_types = [item for sublist in knot_types for item in sublist] #flattens list\n        knot_frequency = Counter(knot_types)\n        common = knot_frequency.most_common()\n        list_common = [list(elem) for elem in common]\n        list_common_fractions = [[elem[0],elem[1]/float(number_of_samples)] for elem in list_common]\n        return list_common_fractions\n\n    def generalised_alexander(self):\n        '''\n        Returns the generalised Alexander polynomial for the default projection\n        of the open knot\n        '''\n\n        gauss_code_crossings = self.gauss_code()._gauss_code[0][:, 0]\n        gauss_code_over_under = Defaults to True, but\n            will work fine if you set it to False (and might even\n            be faster).\n        try_cython : bool\n            Whether to try and use an optimised cython version of the\n            routine (takes about 1/3 of the time for complex\n            representations).  Defaults to True, but the python\n            fallback will be *slower* than setting it to False if the\n            cython function is not available.\n        **kwargs :\n            These are passed directly to :meth:`gauss_code`.\n\n        '''\n        from ..invariants import vassiliev_degree_3\n        gc = self.gauss_code(**kwargs)\n        if simplify:\n            gc.simplify()\n        return vassiliev_degree_3(gc, try_cython=try_cython)\n\n    def hyperbolic_volume(self):\n        '''\n        Returns the hyperbolic volume at the given point, via\n        :meth:`pyknotid.representations.PlanarDiagram.as_spherogram`.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        volume : float\n            A float representing the volume returned.\n        accuracy : int\n            The number of digits of precision. This is significant\n            digits, e.g. 0.00021 with 1 digit precision = 2E-4.\n        solution_type : str\n            The solution type of the manifold. Normally one of:\n            - 'contains degenerate tetrahedra' => may not be a valid result\n            - 'all tetrahedra positively oriented' =>\n              really probably hyperbolic\n        '''\n        from ..invariants import hyperbolic_volume\n        m = self.exterior_manifold()\n        v = m.volume()\n        return (float(v), v.accuracy, m.solution_type())\n\n    def exterior_manifold(self):\n        '''\n        The knot complement manifold of self as a SnapPy class\n        giving access to all of SnapPy's tools.\n\n        This method requires that Spherogram, and possibly SnapPy,\n        are installed.\n        '''\n        link = self.planar_diagram().as_spherogram()\n        link.simplify()  # necessary with snappy 2.5, which can't deal\n                         # with extra Reidemeister moves sometimes\n        return link.exterior()\n\n    def __str__(self):\n        if self._crossings is not None:\n            return '<Knot with {} points, {} crossings>'.format(\n                len(self.points), len(self._crossings))\n        return '<Knot with {} points>'.format(len(self.points))\n\n    def identify(self, determinant=True, alexander=False, roots=(2, 3, 4),\n                 min_crossings=True):\n        '''\n        Provides a simple interface to\n        :func:`pyknotid.catalogue.identify.from_invariants`, by passing\n        the given invariants. This does *not* support all invariants\n        available, or more sophisticated identification methods,\n        so don't be afraid to use the catalogue functions directly.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        determinant : bool\n            If True, uses the knot determinant in the identification.\n            Defaults to True.\n        alexander : bool\n            If True-like, uses the full alexander polynomial in the\n            identification. If the input is a dictionary of kwargs,\n            these are passed straight to self.alexander_polynomial.\n        roots : iterable\n            A list of roots of unity at which to evaluate. Defaults\n            to (2, 3, 4), the first of which is redundant with the\n            determinant. Note that higher roots can be calculated, but\n            aren't available in the database.\n        min_crossings : bool\n            If True, the output is restricted to knots with fewer crossings\n            than the current projection of this one. Defaults to True. The\n            only reason to turn this off is to see what other knots have\n            the same invariants, it is never not useful for direct\n            identification.\n        '''\n        if not roots:\n            roots = []\n        roots = set(roots)\n        if determinant:\n            roots.add(2)\n\n        identify_kwargs = {}\n        for root in roots:\n            identify_kwargs[\n                'alex_imag_{}'.format(root)] = self.alexander_at_root(root)\n\n        if alexander:\n            if not isinstance(alexander, dict):\n                import sympy as sym\n                alexander = {'variable': sym.var('t')}\n            poly = self.alexander_polynomial(**alexander)\n            identify_kwargs['alexander'] = poly\n\n        if min_crossings and len(self.gauss_code()) < 16:\n            identify_kwargs['max_crossings'] = len(self.gauss_code())\n\n        from pyknotid.catalogue.identify import from_invariants\n        return from_invariants(**identify_kwargs)\n\n    def planar_writhe_quantities(self, num_angles=100, **kwargs):\n        '''Returns the second order writhes, and arnold 2St+J+ values, for a\n        range of different projection directions.\n\n        '''\n        from pyknotid.spacecurves.rotation import (\n            get_rotation_angles, rotate_to_top)\n\n        angles = get_rotation_angles(num_angles)\n\n        results = []\n\n        print_dist = int(max(1, 3000. / len(self.points)))\n        # Defaults to 0, which\n            means to sample at all indices.\n        **kwargs :\n            Keyword arguments, passed directly to\n            :meth:`pyknotid.spacecurves.openknot.OpenKnot.alexander_fractions.\n        '''\n        points = self.points\n        if num_samples == 0:\n            num_samples = len(points)\n        indices = n.linspace(0, len(points), num_samples).astype(n.int)\n\n        from pyknotid.spacecurves.openknot import OpenKnot\n\n        arr = n.ones((num_samples, num_samples))\n        for index, points_index in enumerate(indices):\n            self._vprint('\\rindex = {} / {}'.format(index, len(indices)),\n                         False)\n            for other_index, other_points_index in enumerate(\n                    indices[(index+2):]):\n                k = OpenKnot(points[points_index:other_points_index],\n                             verbose=False)\n                if len(k.points) < 4:\n                    alex = 1.\n                else:\n                    alex = k.alexander_fractions(**kwargs)[-1][0]\n                arr[index, other_index] = alex\n\n        import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n        fig, ax = plt.subplots()\n        ax.imshow(arr, interpolation='none')\n\n        ax.plot(n.linspace(0, num_samples, 100) - 0.5,\n                n.linspace(num_samples, 0, 100) - 0.5,\n                color='black',\n                linewidth=3)\n\n        ax.set_xlim(-0.5, num_samples-0.5)\n        ax.set_ylim(-0.4, num_samples-0.5)\n\n        fig.show()\n\n        return fig, ax\n\n    def isolate_knot(self):\n        '''Return indices of self.points within which the knot (if any)\n        appears to lie, according to a simple closure algorithm.\n\n        This method is experimental and may not provide very good results.\n        '''\n        if self._cached_isolated is not None:\n            return self._cached_isolated\n        determinant = self.determinant()\n\n        from pyknotid.spacecurves import OpenKnot\n\n        k1 = OpenKnot(self.points, verbose=False)\n        start, end = _isolate_open_knot(k1, determinant, 0, len(k1))[1:]\n\n        if end - start < 0.6 * len(k1):\n            self._cached_isolated = (start, end)\n            return start, end\n\n        roll_dist = int(0.25*len(self.points))\n        k2 = OpenKnot(n.roll(self.points, roll_dist, axis=0), verbose=False)\n        start, end = _isolate_open_knot(k2, determinant, 0, len(k2))[1:]\n        print('se', start, end)\n        start -= roll_dist\n        start %= len(self)\n        end -= roll_dist\n        end %= len(self)\n        print('now', start, end)\n        self._cached_isolated = (start, end)\n        return start, end\n\n    def plot_isolated(self, **kwargs):\n        '''\n        Plots the curve in red, except for the isolated local knot which\n        is coloured blue. The local knot is found with self.isolate_knot,\n        which may not be reliable or have good resolution.\n\n        Parameters\n        ==========\n        **kwargs :\n            kwargs are passed directly to :meth:`Knot.plot`.\n        '''\n        start, end = self.isolate_knot()\n        if end < start:\n            end, start = start, end\n        mus = n.zeros(len(self.points))\n        mus[start:end+1] = 0.4\n        if end - start > 0.6*len(self) or end == start:\n            mus = 0.4 - mus\n        self.plot(mus=mus, **kwargs)\n\n    def slip_triangle(self, func):\n\n        from \n        for i in range(length + 1):\n            for j in range(length + 1):\n                if i + j >= length:\n                    continue\n\n                new_r = Representation(r)\n\n                points = self.points.copy()\n\n                new_cs = cs.copy()\n\n                new_start = 0\n                new_end = -1\n                for _ in range(i):\n results[(i, j)] = new_points\n                invs[(i, j)] = func(new_r)\n\n        import matplotlib.pyplot as plt\n        from matplotlib.gridspec import GridSpec\n\n        points = self.points\n whitney_index(self):\n        '''The degree of the Gauss map mapping a point on the curve to the\n        direction of the positive tangent vector at this point.'''\n\n        points = self.points\n        tangents = self.tangents()\n\n        directions = np.apply_along_axis(\n            lambda arr: np.arctan2(arr[1], arr[0]), 1, tangents)\n\n        directions[directions > np.pi] -= 2*np.pi\n        directions[directions < -np.pi] += 2*np.pi\n\n        jumps = np.roll(directions, -1) - directions\n\n\n        index = np.sum(jumps > np.pi) - np.sum(jumps < -np.pi)\n\n        return index\n\n    def arnold_2St_2Jplus(self, **kwargs):\n        from pyknotid.invariants import arnold_2St_2Jplus\n        gc = self.gauss_code(**kwargs)\n        # Do *not* simplify, as this is only a plane curve invariant\n        return arnold_2St_2Jplus(gc)\n\n    def arnold_2St_2Jminus(self, **kwargs):\n        from pyknotid.invariants import arnold_2St_2Jminus\n        gc = self.gauss_code(**kwargs)\n        # Do *not* simplify, as this is only a plane curve invariant\n        return arnold_2St_2Jminus(gc)\n\n    def plot_secant_manifold(self, plot_extrema=True):\n        import matplotlib.pyplot as plot_secant_crossings(self, radius=None, **kwargs):\n        \n        crossings = self.raw_crossings(**kwargs)\n\n        if radius is None:\n            radii = self.points - np.average(self.points, axis=0)\n            radius = 2.5 * np.max(np.sqrt(np.sum(radii**2, axis=1)))\n\n        unique_crossings = []\n        crossings_done = set()\n        for crossing in crossings:\n            if crossing[0] in crossings_done:\n                continue\n            crossings_done.add(crossing[1])\n            unique_crossings.append(crossing)\n\n        points = self.points\n\n        lines = []\n\n        for i, crossing in enumerate(unique_crossings):\n            start1, end1, height1, sign1 = crossing\n            for other_crossing in unique_crossings[i+1:]:\n                start2, points[int(start1)]\n                start_point1 += (start1 - int(start1)) * (\n                    points[int(start1) + 1] - points[int(start1)])\n                segment_point1s = points[int(start1) + 1:int(start2) + 1]\n                end_point1 = points[int(start2)]\n                end_point1 += (start2 - int(start2)) * (\n                    points[int(start2) + 1] - points[int(start2)])\n\n                segment1 = np.vstack(\n                    [start_point1, segment_point1s, end_point1])\n\n                #\n                start_point2 = points[int(end1)]\n                start_point2 += (end1 - int(end1)) * (\n                    points[int(end1) + 1] - points[int(end1)])\n                segment_point2s = points[int(end1) + 1:int(end2) + 1]\n                end_point2 = points[int(end2)]\n                end_point2 += (end2 - int(end2)) * (\n                    points[int(end2) + 1] - points[int(end2)])\n\n                segment2 = np.vstack(\n                    [start_point2, segment_point2s, end_point2])\n\n                directions = np.vstack([\n                    start_point1 - segment2,\n                    segment1 - end_point2])\n\n                lines.append(directions)\n\n        sphere_lines = []\n\n        for i, line in enumerate(lines):\n            radii = np.sqrt(np.sum(line**2, axis=1))\n            thetas = np.arccos(line[:, 2] / radii)\n            phis = np.arctan2(line[:, 1], line[:, 0])\n\n            local_radius = radius - 0.02*i*radius*np.sin(2*np.sin(thetas))\n\n            sphere_points = self.plot(tube_radius=0.1, colour=(0.3, 0.3, 0.3, 1))\n\n        # Plot the poles\n        from vispy.geometry import create_sphere\n        from vispy.scene import Mesh\n        meshdata = OpenKnot(line)\n            k.plot(clf=False, tube_radius=0.15, colour=colour, zero_centroid=False)\n\n\ndef mag(v):\n    return n.sqrt(v.dot(v))\n\n\ndef _isolate_open_knot(k, det, start, end):\n    from pyknotid.spacecurves import OpenKnot\n    if len(k.points) < 10:  \n        return (False, None, None)\n\n    alexanders = k.alexander_fractions()\n    main_det, main_frac = alexanders[-1]\n\n\n    if main_det != det:\n        return (False, None, None)\n\n    half_len = len(k.points) // 2\n    k1 = OpenKnot(k.points[:half_len], verbose=False)\n    k1_knotted, k1_start, k1_end = _isolate_open_knot(\n        k1, det, start, start + half_len - 1)\n    if k1_knotted:\n        return (True, k1_start, k1_end)\n\n    k2 = OpenKnot(k.points[half_len:], verbose=False)\n    k2_knotted, k2_start, k2_end = _isolate_open_knot(\n        k2, det, start + half_len, end)\n    if k2_knotted:\n        return (True, k2_start, k2_end)\n\n    quarter_len = len(k.points) // 4\n    k3 = OpenKnot(k.points[quarter_len:(quarter_len + half_len)], verbose=False)\n    k3_knotted, k3_start, k3_end = _isolate_open_knot(\n        k3, det, start + quarter_len, start + quarter_len + quadrant='lr'):\n    '''\n    Numpy implementation of the Alexander polynomial (evaluated\n    at a float), assuming the input has been sanitised by\n    :func:`alexander`.\n    '''\n    import numpy jones_polynomial(representation, var=q):\n    from pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    from pyknotid.representations.planardiagram import PlanarDiagram, Crossing\n    if not isinstance(representation, (GaussCode, PlanarDiagram)):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n    if isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = PlanarDiagram(representation)\n\n    rep = representation\n\n    if isinstance(q, sym.Symbol):\n        A = sym.var('A')\n    else:\n        A = (var + 0j)**0.25\n    \n    crossings = crossings_connectedness_order(rep)\n    if len(crossings) < 2:\n        return 1\n    \n    web = []\n    crossing = crossings[0]\n    crossing = PlanarDiagram(crossing)\n    diag_1, diag_2 = kauffman_skein(crossing)\n    web.append([diag_1, A])\n    web.append([diag_2, 1.0/A])\n    replacement = -A**2 - A**-2\n\n    for crossing in crossings[1:]:\n        crossing = PlanarDiagram(crossing)\n        diag_1, diag_2 = kauffman_skein(crossing)\n        new_web = []\n\n        for term in web:\n            diag_1_product = PlanarDiagram(\n                [x for x in diag_1] +\n                [x for x in term[0]]).contract_points()\n            diag_2_product = PlanarDiagram(\n                [x for x in diag_2] +\n                [x for x in term[0]]).contract_points()\n            contracted_1 = 0\n            contracted_2 = 0\n            diag_1_new = []\n            diag_2_new = []\n\n            for point in diag_1_product:\n                if len(point.components()) == 1:\n                    contracted_1 += 1\n                else:\n                    diag_1_new.append(point)\n            for point in diag_2_product:\n                if len(point.components()) == 1:\n                    contracted_2 += 1\n                else:\n                    diag_2_new.append(point)\n\n            new_term_1 = ([sorted(diag_1_new)] +\n                          [term[1] * (replacement ** contracted_1) * A])\n            new_term_2 = ([sorted(diag_2_new)] +\n                          [term[1] * (replacement ** contracted_2) * 1.0/A])\n\n            new_web.append(new_term_1)\n            new_web.append(new_term_2)\n\n        unique_diags = list(set([tuple(x[0]) for x in new_web]))\n        contracted_web = [[list(x)] for x in unique_diags]\n\n        for term in new_web:\n            for diag in contracted_web:\n                if diag[0] == term[0]:\n                    if len(diag) == 1:\n                        diag.append(term[1])\n                    else:\n                        diag[1] += term[1]\n\n        web = contracted_web\n\n    # quick and dirty writhe calculation\n    crossings = [x for x in rep if isinstance(x, Crossing)]\n    total_writhe = 0\n    for crossing in crossings:\n        total_writhe += crossing.sign()\n        \n    jones_precursor = web[0][1]\n\n    jones_poly = (-A**3)**total_writhe * jones_precursor / replacement\n\n    if isinstance(var, sym.Symbol):\n        return sym.expand(sym.simplify(jones_poly)).subs(A, var**0.25)\n    return round(abs(jones_poly))\n\n\ndef crossings_connectedness_order(planar_diagram):\n    from pyknotid.representations.planardiagram import Crossing\n    crossings = [x for x in planar_diagram if isinstance(x, Crossing)]\n\n    inner_components = crossings[0].components()\n    crossings_remaining = crossings[1:]\n    ordered_crossings = [crossings[0]]\n\n    for i in range(len(crossings_remaining)):\n        connectedness = []\n        for crossing in crossings_remaining:\n            new_components = (len(list(set(crossing.components() + inner_components))) -\n                              len(inner_components))\n\n            connectedness.append(len(crossing.components()) - new_components)\n        most_connected_index = connectedness.index(max(connectedness))\n        inner_components += crossings_remaining[most_connected_index].components()\n        inner_components = list(set(inner_components))\n        ordered_crossings.append(crossings_remaining[most_connected_index])\n        del crossings_remaining[most_connected_index]\n\n    return ordered_crossings\n\ndef contract_points(planar_diagram):\n    '''\n    For appropriately contracting :class: `Points` in a\n    :class: `PlanarDiagram` According to the following rules:\n\n    P_a,b P_b,c -> P_a,c\n    P_a,b P_a,b -> P_a,a\n    '''\n    import copy\n\n    pd_crossings = [x for x in planar_diagram if type(x) == Crossing]\n    pd_points = [x for x in planar_diagram if type(x) == Point]\n\n    pd_trimmed = PlanarDiagram()\n    for crossing in pd_crossings:\n        pd_trimmed.append(crossing)\n\n    substituted_points = []\n    for i in range(len(pd_points)):\n        if pd_points[i] not in substituted_points:\n            pd_trimmed.append(pd_points[i])\n            totally_simplified = False\n            while not totally_simplified:\n                pd_trimmed_old = copy.copy(pd_trimmed)\n                for j in range(i+1, len(pd_points)):\n                    if (len(pd_trimmed[-1].components()) > 1 and\n                            len(pd_points[j].components()) > 1 and\n                            pd_points[j] not in substituted_points):\n                        # This skips P_a,a situations\n                        if pd_trimmed[-1].components() != pd_points[j].components():\n                            # This conditions skips (P_a,b P_a,b) situations\n                            substituted = False\n                            p = 0\n                            while substituted is not True and p != 2:\n                                q = 0\n                                while substituted is not True and q != 2:\n                                    if pd_trimmed[-1].components()[p] == pd_points[j].components()[q]:\n                                        pd_trimmed.append(Point(pd_trimmed[-1].components()[(p+1) % 2],\n                                                                pd_points[j].components()[(q+1) % 2]))\n                                        del pd_trimmed[-2]\n                                        substituted = True\n                                        substituted_points.append(pd_points[j])\n                                    q += 1\n                                p += 1\n                        else:\n                            # This deals with (P_a,b P_a,b) situations\n                            pd_trimmed.append(Point(pd_trimmed[-1].components()[0],\n                                                    pd_trimmed[-1].components()[0]))\n                            del pd_trimmed[-2]\n                            substituted_points.append(pd_points[j])\n                if pd_trimmed_old == pd_trimmed:\n                    totally_simplified = True\n    return pd_trimmed\n\n\ndef _write_mathematica_script(filen, text):\n    '''\n    Write the given text (mathematica code) to the given Gauss diagram) and signs, both in dictionaries.'''\n\n    ## Arrow diagram for v_2 is\n    ## appear -> leave -> leave -> appear (with crossed arrows)\n    over_crossing_indices = {}\n    under_crossing_indices = {}\n    signs = {}\n    for i, row in enumerate(gc):\n        if row[1] == 1:\n            over_crossing_indices[row[0]] = i\n        else:\n            under_crossing_indices[row[0]] = i\n        signs[row[0]] = row[2]\n\n    arrows = {index: (over_crossing_indices[index],\n                      under_crossing_indices[index])\n              for index crossed arrows)\n    over_crossing_indices = {}\n    under_crossing_indices = {}\n    signs = {}\n    for i, row in enumerate(gc):\n        if row[1] == 1:\n            over_crossing_indices[row[0]] = i\n        else:\n            under_crossing_indices[row[0]] = i\n        signs[row[0]] = row[2]\n\n    arrows = n.zeros((len(crossing_numbers), 3), dtype=n.long)\n\n    for index, number in enumerate(crossing_numbers):\n        row = arrows[index]\n        row[0] = over_crossing_indices[number]\n        row[1] = under_crossing_indices[number]\n        row[2] = signs[number]\n\n    return arrows\n\ndef second_order_writhe(representation):\n    '''Returns the second order writhe (i1,i3,i2,i4) of the\n    representation, as defined in Lin and Wang.\n    '''\n\n    from pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    if not isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n\n    gc = representation._gauss_code\n    if len(gc) == 0:\n        return 0\n    elif len(gc) > 1:\n        raise Exception('tried to calculate second order writhe'\n                        'for something with more than 1 component')\n\n    gc = gc[0].copy()\n\n    arrows, signs = _crossing_arrows_and_signs(\n        gc, representation.crossing_numbers)\n    \n    crossing_numbers = list(representation.crossing_numbers)\n    result = 0\n    for index, i1 in enumerate(crossing_numbers):\n        arrow1 = arrows[i1]\n        a1s, a1e = arrow1\n        for i2 in crossing_numbers[index+1:]:\n            arrow2 = arrows[i2]\n            a2s, a2e = arrow2\n\n            if ((a2s > a1e and a1s > a2s and a2e > a1s) or\n                (a2e > a1e and a1s > a2e and a2s > a1s) or\n                (a2e > a1s and a1e > a2e and a2s > a1e) or\n                (a2s > a1s and a1e > a2s and a2e > a1e)):\n            # if ((a2e > a1e and a1s > a2e and a2s > a1s) or\n            #     (a2s > a1s and a1e > a2s and a2e > a1e)):\n calculation is performed by transforming the representation\n    into a representation of an unknot by flipping crossings, then\n    calculating the second order writhe.\n\n    See 'Invariants of curves and fronts via Gauss diagrams', M\n    Polyak, Topology 37, 1998.\n\n    '''\n\n    from pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    if not isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n\n    gc = representation._gauss_code\n    if len(gc) == 0:\n        return 0\n    elif len(gc) > 1:\n        raise Exception('tried to calculate arnold_2St_2Jplus'\n                        'for something with more than 1 component')\n\n    gc = gc[0].copy()\n    seen_crossings = set()\n    changed_crossings = set()\n    for row in gc:\n        if row[0] not in seen_crossings:\n            seen_crossings.add(row[0])\n            if row[1] < 0:\n                changed_crossings.add(row[0])\n                row[1] *= -1\n                row[2] *= -1\n        elif row[0] in changed_crossings:\n            row[1] *= -1\n            row[2] *= -1\n    arrows, signs = _crossing_arrows_and_signs(\n        gc, representation.crossing_numbers)\n    \n    crossing_numbers = list(representation.crossing_numbers)\n    result = 0\n    for index, i1 in enumerate(crossing_numbers):\n        arrow1 = arrows[i1]\n        a1s, a1e = arrow1\n        for i2 in crossing_numbers[index+1:]:\n            arrow2 = arrows[i2]\n            a2s, a2e = arrow2\n\n            if ((a2s > a1e and a1s > a2s and a2e > a1s) or\n                (a2e > a1e and a1s > a2e and a2s > a1s) or\n                (a2e > a1s and a1e > a2e and a2s > a1e) or\n                (a2s > a1s and a1e > a2s and a2e > a1e)):\n            # if ((a2e > a1e and a1s > a2e and a2s > a1s) or\n            #     (a2s > a1s and a1e > a2s and a2e > a1e)):\n                result += signs[i1] * signs[i2]\n\n    return result\n\ndef Polyak, Topology 37, 1998.\n\n    '''\n    return arnold_2St_2Jplus(representation) - len(representation)\n\ndef vassiliev_degree_2(representation):\n    '''Calculates the Vassiliev invariant of a1s) % len(gc)\n        for i2 in crossing_numbers:\n            arrow2 = arrows[i2]\n            a2s, a2e = arrow2\n            a2s = (a2s - a1s) % len(gc)\n            a2e = (a2e - a1s) % len(gc)\n            for i3 in crossing_numbers:\n                arrow3 = arrows[i3]\n                a3s, a3e = arrow3\n                a3s = (a3s - See Kauffman 2004 for more\n    information.\n\n    Currently only works for knots.\n    '''\n\n    from pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    if not isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n\n    if len(representation) == 0:\n        return 0\n    if len(representation._gauss_code[0]) == 0:\n        return 0\n    gauss_code = representation._gauss_code\n    l = len(gauss_code[0][:,0])\n    total_crossings = l/2\n    crossing_counter = 1\n    slink_counter = 0        \n\n    for crossing_number in representation.crossing_numbers:\n        occurences = n.where(gauss_code[0][:, 0] == crossing_number)[0]\n        first_occurence = occurences[0]\n        second_occurence = occurences[1]\n        crossing_difference = second_occurence - first_occurence        \n\n        if(crossing_difference % 2 == 0):\n            slink_counter += 2 * gauss_code[0][occurences[0],2]\n\n    return slink_counter   \n\ndef virtual_vassiliev_degree_3(representation):\n    '''Calculates the virtual Vassiliev invariant of degree 3 (for\n    non-long-knots) of the given representation, as described in\n    'Finite type invariants of classical and virtual knots' by\n    Goussarov, Polyak and Viro.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    representation : :class:`~pyknotid.representations.representation.Representation`\n        A representation class, or anything convertible to one\n        (in principle).\n    '''\n    ## See Polyak and Viro and Goussarov\n    from pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    if not isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n\n    gc = representation._gauss_code\n    if len(gc) == 0:\n        return 0\n    elif len(gc) > 1:\n        raise Exception('tried to calculate alexander polynomial'\n                        'for something with more than 1 component')\n\n    gc = gc[0]\n    arrows, signs = _crossing_arrows_and_signs(\n        gc, representation.crossing_numbers)\n\n    diagrams_found = [[] for _ in range(8)]\n    \n    crossing_numbers = list(representation.crossing_numbers)\n    used_sets = set()\n    representations_sum = 0\n    for index, i1 in enumerate(crossing_numbers):\n        if index % 10 == 0 and len(crossing_numbers) > 10:\n            vprint('\\rCurrently comparing index {}'.format(index), False)\n        arrow1 = arrows[i1]\n        a1s, a1e = arrow1\n        a1e = (a1e - a1s) % len(gc)\n        for i2 in crossing_numbers:\n            arrow2 = arrows[i2]\n            a2s, a2e = arrow2\n            a2s = (a2s - a1s) % len(gc)\n            a2e = (a2e - a1s) % len(gc)\n\n            # For the virtual Vassilev of order f, there are still\n            # some diagrams with just 2 arrows:\n            first_two_ordered_indices = tuple(sorted((i1, i2)))\n            if (a2s < a1e and a2e > a1e and\n                signs[i1] == signs[i2] and\n                first_two_ordered_indices not in used_sets):\n                if signs[i1] > 0:\n                    representations_sum -= 1\n                    # print('7')\n                else:\n                    representations_sum += 1\n                    # print('8')\n                used_sets.add(first_two_ordered_indices)\n           \n            for i3 in crossing_numbers:\n                arrow3 = arrows[i3]\n                a3s, a3e = arrow3\n                a3s = (a3s - a1s) % len(gc)\n                a3e = (a3e - a1s) % len(gc)\n\n                ordered_indices = tuple(sorted((i1, i2, i3)))\n                if ordered_indices in used_sets:\n                    continue\n\n                # These checks are for the jones_polynomial(representation, var=q):\n    from pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    from pyknotid.representations.planardiagram import PlanarDiagram, Crossing\n    if not isinstance(representation, (GaussCode, PlanarDiagram)):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n    if isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = PlanarDiagram(representation)\n\n    rep = representation\n\n    if isinstance(q, sym.Symbol):\n        A = sym.var('A')\n    else:\n        A = (var + 0j)**0.25\n    \n    crossings = crossings_connectedness_order(rep)\n    if len(crossings) < 2:\n        return 1\n    \n    web = []\n    crossing = crossings[0]\n    crossing = PlanarDiagram(crossing)\n    diag_1, diag_2 = kauffman_skein(crossing)\n    web.append([diag_1, A])\n    web.append([diag_2, 1.0/A])\n    replacement = -A**2 - A**-2\n\n    for crossing in crossings[1:]:\n        crossing = PlanarDiagram(crossing)\n        diag_1, diag_2 = kauffman_skein(crossing)\n        new_web = []\n\n        for term in web:\n            diag_1_product = PlanarDiagram(\n                [x for x in diag_1] +\n                [x for x in term[0]]).contract_points()\n            diag_2_product = PlanarDiagram(\n                [x for x in diag_2] +\n                [x for x in term[0]]).contract_points()\n            contracted_1 = 0\n            contracted_2 = 0\n            diag_1_new = []\n            diag_2_new = []\n\n            for point in diag_1_product:\n                if len(point.components()) == 1:\n                    contracted_1 += 1\n                else:\n                    diag_1_new.append(point)\n            for point in diag_2_product:\n                if len(point.components()) == 1:\n                    contracted_2 += 1\n                else:\n                    diag_2_new.append(point)\n\n            new_term_1 = ([sorted(diag_1_new)] +\n                          [term[1] * (replacement ** contracted_1) * A])\n            new_term_2 = ([sorted(diag_2_new)] +\n                          [term[1] * (replacement ** contracted_2) * 1.0/A])\n\n            new_web.append(new_term_1)\n            new_web.append(new_term_2)\n\n        unique_diags = list(set([tuple(x[0]) for x in new_web]))\n        contracted_web = [[list(x)] for x in unique_diags]\n\n        for term in new_web:\n            for diag in contracted_web:\n                if diag[0] == term[0]:\n                    if len(diag) == 1:\n                        diag.append(term[1])\n                    else:\n                        diag[1] += term[1]\n\n        web = contracted_web\n\n    # quick and dirty writhe calculation\n    crossings = [x for x in rep (-A**3)**total_writhe * jones_precursor / replacement\n\n    if isinstance(var, sym.Symbol):\n        return sym.expand(sym.simplify(jones_poly)).subs(A, var**0.25)\n    return isinstance(x, Crossing)]\n\n    inner_components = crossings[0].components()\n    crossings_remaining = crossings[1:]\n    ordered_crossings = [crossings[0]]\n\n    for i in range(len(crossings_remaining)):\n        connectedness = []\n        for crossing in crossings_remaining:\n            new_components = (len(list(set(crossing.components() + inner_components))) -\n                              len(inner_components))\n\n            connectedness.append(len(crossing.components()) - new_components)\n        most_connected_index = connectedness.index(max(connectedness))\n        inner_components += crossings_remaining[most_connected_index].components()\n        inner_components = list(set(inner_components))\n        ordered_crossings.append(crossings_remaining[most_connected_index])\n        del crossings_remaining[most_connected_index]\n\n    return ordered_crossings\n\ndef contract_points(planar_diagram):\n    '''\n    For appropriately contracting :class: `Points` in a\n    :class: `PlanarDiagram` According to the following rules:\n\n    P_a,b P_b,c -> P_a,c\n    P_a,b P_a,b -> P_a,a\n    '''\n    import copy\n\n    pd_crossings = [x for x in planar_diagram if type(x) == Crossing]\n    pd_points = [x for x in planar_diagram if type(x) == Point]\n\n    pd_trimmed = PlanarDiagram()\n    for crossing in pd_crossings:\n        pd_trimmed.append(crossing)\n\n    substituted_points = []\n    for i in range(len(pd_points)):\n        if pd_points[i] not in substituted_points:\n            pd_trimmed.append(pd_points[i])\n            totally_simplified = False\n            while not totally_simplified:\n                pd_trimmed_old = copy.copy(pd_trimmed)\n                for j in range(i+1, len(pd_points)):\n                    if (len(pd_trimmed[-1].components()) > 1 and\n                            len(pd_points[j].components()) > 1 and\n                            pd_points[j] not in substituted_points):\n                        # This skips P_a,a situations\n                        if pd_trimmed[-1].components() != pd_points[j].components():\n                            # This conditions skips (P_a,b P_a,b) situations\n                            substituted = False\n                            p = 0\n                            while substituted is not True and p != 2:\n                                q = 0\n                                while substituted is not True and q != 2:\n                                    if pd_trimmed[-1].components()[p] == pd_points[j].components()[q]:\n                                        pd_trimmed.append(Point(pd_trimmed[-1].components()[(p+1) % 2],\n                                                                pd_points[j].components()[(q+1) % 2]))\n                                        del pd_trimmed[-2]\n                                        substituted = True\n                                        substituted_points.append(pd_points[j])\n                                    q += 1\n                                p += 1\n                        else:\n                            # This deals with (P_a,b P_a,b) situations\n                            pd_trimmed.append(Point(pd_trimmed[-1].components()[0],\n                                                    pd_trimmed[-1].components()[0]))\n                            del pd_trimmed[-2]\n                            substituted_points.append(pd_points[j])\n                if pd_trimmed_old == pd_trimmed:\n                    totally_simplified = True\n    return pd_trimmed\n\n\ndef _write_mathematica_script(filen, text):\n    '''\n    Write the given text (mathematica code) to the given Gauss diagram) and signs, both in dictionaries.'''\n\n    ## Arrow diagram for v_2 is\n    ## appear -> leave -> leave -> appear (with crossed arrows)\n    over_crossing_indices = {}\n    under_crossing_indices = {}\n    signs = {}\n    for i, row in enumerate(gc):\n        if row[1] == 1:\n            over_crossing_indices[row[0]] = i\n        else:\n            under_crossing_indices[row[0]] = i\n        signs[row[0]] = row[2]\n\n    arrows = {index: (over_crossing_indices[index],\n                      under_crossing_indices[index])\n              for index in crossing_numbers}\n\n    return arrows, signs\n\ndef _crossing_arrows_and_signs_numpy(gc, crossing_numbers):\n    ## Arrow diagram for v_2 is\n    ## appear -> leave -> leave -> appear (with crossed arrows)\n    over_crossing_indices = {}\n    under_crossing_indices = {}\n    signs = {}\n    for i, row in enumerate(gc):\n        if row[1] == 1:\n            over_crossing_indices[row[0]] = i\n        else:\n            under_crossing_indices[row[0]] = i\n        signs[row[0]] = row[2]\n\n    arrows = n.zeros((len(crossing_numbers), 3), dtype=n.long)\n\n    for index, number in enumerate(crossing_numbers):\n        row = arrows[index]\n        row[0] = over_crossing_indices[number]\n        row[1] = under_crossing_indices[number]\n        row[2] = signs[number]\n\n    return arrows\n\ndef second_order_writhe(representation):\n    '''Returns the second order writhe (i1,i3,i2,i4) of the\n    representation, as defined in Lin and Wang.\n    '''\n\n    from pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    if not isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n\n    gc = representation._gauss_code\n    if len(gc) == 0:\n        return 0\n    elif len(gc) > 1:\n        raise Exception('tried to calculate second order writhe'\n                        'for something with more than 1 component')\n\n    gc = gc[0].copy()\n\n    arrows, signs = _crossing_arrows_and_signs(\n        gc, representation.crossing_numbers)\n    \n    crossing_numbers = list(representation.crossing_numbers)\n    result = 0\n    for index, i1 in enumerate(crossing_numbers):\n        arrow1 = arrows[i1]\n        a1s, a1e = arrow1\n        for i2 in crossing_numbers[index+1:]:\n            arrow2 = arrows[i2]\n            a2s, a2e = arrow2\n\n            if ((a2s > a1e and a1s > a2s and a2e > a1s) or\n                (a2e > a1e and a1s > a2e and a2s > a1s) or\n                (a2e > a1s and a1e > a2e and a2s > a1e) or\n                (a2s > a1s and a1e > a2s and a2e > a1e)):\n            # if ((a2e > a1e and a1s > a2e and a2s > a1s) or\n            #     (a2s > a1s and a1e > a2s and a2e > a1e)):\n                result += signs[i1] * signs[i2]\n\n    return result\n\ndef arnold_2St_2Jplus(representation):\n    '''Returns J+ + 2 * St where J+ and St are Arnold's invariants of\n    plane curves.\n\n    The calculation is performed by transforming the representation\n    into a representation of an unknot by flipping crossings, then\n    calculating the second order writhe.\n\n    See 'Invariants of curves and fronts via Gauss diagrams', M\n    Polyak, Topology 37, 1998.\n\n    '''\n\n    from pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    if not isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n\n    gc = representation._gauss_code\n    if len(gc) == 0:\n        return 0\n    elif len(gc) > 1:\n        raise Exception('tried to calculate arnold_2St_2Jplus'\n                        'for something with more than 1 component')\n\n    gc = gc[0].copy()\n    seen_crossings = set()\n    changed_crossings = set()\n    for row in gc:\n        if row[0] not in seen_crossings:\n            seen_crossings.add(row[0])\n            if row[1] < 0:\n                changed_crossings.add(row[0])\n                row[1] *= -1\n                row[2] *= -1\n        elif row[0] in changed_crossings:\n            row[1] *= -1\n            row[2] *= -1\n    arrows, signs = _crossing_arrows_and_signs(\n        gc, representation.crossing_numbers)\n    \n    crossing_numbers = list(representation.crossing_numbers)\n    result = 0\n    for index, i1 in enumerate(crossing_numbers):\n        arrow1 = arrows[i1]\n        a1s, a1e = arrow1\n        for i2 in crossing_numbers[index+1:]:\n            arrow2 = arrows[i2]\n            a2s, a2e = arrow2\n\n            if ((a2s > a1e and a1s > a2s and a2e > a1s) or\n                (a2e > a1e and a1s > a2e and a2s > a1s) or\n                (a2e > a1s and a1e > a2e and a2s > a1e) or\n                (a2s > a1s and a1e > a2s and a2e > a1e)):\n            # if ((a2e > a1e and a1s > a2e and a2s > a1s) or\n            #     (a2s > a1s and a1e > a2s and a2e > a1e)):\n                result += signs[i1] * signs[i2]\n\n    return result\n\ndef Polyak, Topology 37, 1998.\n\n    '''\n    return arnold_2St_2Jplus(representation) - len(representation)\n\ndef vassiliev_degree_2(representation):\n    '''Calculates the Vassiliev invariant of degree 2 of the given\n    knot. The representation must have just one knot component,\n    this doesn't work for links.\n\n    Parameters\n    ==========\n    representation : Anything convertible to a\n                     :class:`~pyknotid.representations.gausscode.GaussCode`\n        A pyknotid representation class for the knot, or anything that\n        can automatically be converted into a GaussCode (i.e. by writing\n        :code:`GaussCode(your_object)`).\n    '''\n    ## See Polyak and Viro\n    from pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    if not isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n\n    gc = representation._gauss_code\n    if len(gc) == 0:\n        return 0\n    elif len(gc) > 1:\n        raise Exception('tried to calculate v2'\n                        'for something with more than 1 component')\n\n    gc = gc[0]\n    arrows, signs = _crossing_arrows_and_signs(\n        gc, representation.crossing_numbers)\n    \n    crossing_numbers = list(representation.crossing_numbers)\n    representations_sum = 0\n    for index, i1 in enumerate(crossing_numbers):\n        arrow1 = arrows[i1]\n        a1s, a1e = arrow1\n        for i2 in crossing_numbers[index+1:]:\n            arrow2 = arrows[i2]\n            a2s, a2e = arrow2\n\n            if a2s > a1s and a2e < a1s and a1e > a2s:\n                representations_sum += signs[i1] * signs[i2]\n            elif a1s > a2s and a1e < a2s and a2e > a1s:\n                representations_sum += signs[i1] * signs[i2]\n\n    return representations_sum\n\ndef vassiliev_degree_3(representation, try_cython=True):\n    '''Calculates the Vassiliev invariant of degree 3 of the given\n    knot. The representation must have just one knot component,\n    this doesn't work for links.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    representation : Anything convertible to a\n                     :class:`~pyknotid.representations.gausscode.GaussCode`\n        A pyknotid representation class for the knot, or anything that\n        can automatically be converted into a GaussCode (i.e. by writing\n        :code:`GaussCode(your_object)`).\n    try_cython : bool\n        Whether to try and use an optimised cython version of the\n        routine (takes about 1/3 of the time for complex representations).\n        Defaults to True, but the python fallback will be *slower*\n        than setting it to False if the cython function is not\n        available.\n    '''\n\n    if try_cython:\n        return _vassiliev_degree_3_numpy(representation)\n    return _vassiliev_degree_3_python(representation)\n\n        \ndef _vassiliev_degree_3_python(representation):\n    ## See Polyak and Viro\n    from pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    if not isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n\n    gc = representation._gauss_code\n    if len(gc) == 0:\n        return 0\n    elif len(gc) > 1:\n        raise Exception('tried to calculate alexander polynomial'\n                        'for something with more than 1 component')\n\n    gc = gc[0]\n    arrows, signs = _crossing_arrows_and_signs(\n        gc, representation.crossing_numbers)\n    \n    crossing_numbers = list(representation.crossing_numbers)\n    used_sets = set()\n    representations_sum_1 = 0\n    representations_sum_2 = 0\n    for index, i1 in enumerate(crossing_numbers):\n        if index % 10 == 0:\n            vprint('\\rCurrently comparing index {}'.format(index), False)\n        arrow1 = arrows[i1]\n        a1s, a1e = arrow1\n        a1e = (a1e - a1s) % len(gc)\n        for i2 in crossing_numbers:\n            arrow2 = arrows[i2]\n            a2s, a2e = arrow2\n            a2s = (a2s - a1s) % len(gc)\n            a2e = (a2e - a1s) % len(gc)\n            for i3 in crossing_numbers:\n                arrow3 = arrows[i3]\n                a3s, a3e = arrow3\n                a3s = (a3s - a1s) % len(gc)\n                a3e = (a3e - a1s) % len(gc)\n\n                ordered_indices = tuple(sorted((i1, i2, i3)))\n                if ordered_indices in used_sets:\n                    continue\n\n                if (a2s < a1e and a3e < a1e and a3e > a2s and\n                    a3s > a1e and a2e > a3s):\n                    representations_sum_1 += (signs[i1] * signs[i2] *\n                                              signs[i3])\n                    used_sets.add(ordered_indices)\n                if (a2e < a1e and a3s < a1e and a3s > a2e and\n                    a2s > a1e and a3e > a2s):\n                    representations_sum_2 += (signs[i1] * signs[i2] *\n                                              signs[i3])\n                    used_sets.add(ordered_indices)\n\n    print()\n    \n    return int(round(representations_sum_1 / 2.)) + representations_sum_2\n\n    \ndef _vassiliev_degree_3_numpy(representation):\n    ## See Polyak and Viro\n    from pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    if not isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n\n    gc = representation._gauss_code\n    if len(gc) == 0:\n        return 0\n    elif len(gc) > 1:\n        raise Exception('tried to calculate alexander polynomial'\n                        'for something with more than 1 component')\n\n    gc = gc[0]\n    arrows = _crossing_arrows_and_signs_numpy(\n        gc, representation.crossing_numbers)\n\n    try:\n        from pyknotid import cinvariants\n    except ImportError:\n        print('Failed to import cinvariants. Using *slow* python numpy method.')\n    else:\n        return int(round(cinvariants.vassiliev_degree_3(arrows)))\n\n    num_crossings = len(arrows) * 2\n    \n    used_sets = set()\n    representations_sum_1 = 0\n    representations_sum_2 = 0\n    arrow_range = range(len(arrows))\n    for i1 in arrow_range:\n        if i1 % 10 == 0:\n            vprint('\\rCurrently comparing index {}   '.format(i1), False)\n        arrow1 = arrows[i1]\n        a1s, a1e, sign1 = arrow1\n        a1e = (a1e - a1s) % num_crossings\n        for i2 in arrow_range:\n            arrow2 = arrows[i2]\n            a2s, a2e, sign2 = arrow2\n            a2s = (a2s - a1s) % num_crossings\n            a2e = (a2e - a1s) % num_crossings\n            for i3 in arrow_range:\n                arrow3 = arrows[i3]\n                a3s, a3e, sign3 = arrow3\n                a3s = (a3s - a1s) % num_crossings\n                a3e = (a3e - a1s) % num_crossings\n\n                ordered_indices = tuple(sorted((i1, i2, i3)))\n                if ordered_indices in used_sets:\n                    continue\n\n                if (a2s < a1e and a3e < a1e and a3e > a2s and\n                    a3s > a1e and a2e > a3s):\n                    representations_sum_1 += sign1 * sign2 * sign3\n                    used_sets.add(ordered_indices)\n                if (a2e < a1e and a3s < a1e and a3s > a2e and\n                    a2s > a1e and a3e > a2s):\n                    representations_sum_2 += sign1 * sign2 * sign3\n                    used_sets.add(ordered_indices)\n\n    print\n                    \n    \n    return int(round(representations_sum_1 / 2.)) + representations_sum_2\n\n\ndef self_linking(representation):\n    '''Returns the self linking number J(K) of the Gauss code, an\n    invariant of virtual knots. See Kauffman 2004 for more\n    information.\n\n    Currently only works for knots.\n    '''\n\n    from pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    if not isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n\n    if len(representation) == 0:\n        return 0\n    if len(representation._gauss_code[0]) == 0:\n        return 0\n    gauss_code = representation._gauss_code\n    l = len(gauss_code[0][:,0])\n    total_crossings = l/2\n    crossing_counter = 1\n    slink_counter = 0        \n\n    for crossing_number in representation.crossing_numbers:\n        occurences = n.where(gauss_code[0][:, 0] == crossing_number)[0]\n        first_occurence = occurences[0]\n        second_occurence = occurences[1]\n        crossing_difference = second_occurence - first_occurence        \n\n        if(crossing_difference % 2 == 0):\n            slink_counter += 2 * gauss_code[0][occurences[0],2]\n\n    return                            len(inner_components))\n\n            connectedness.append(len(crossing.components()) - new_components)\n        most_connected_index = '''\n    For appropriately contracting :class: `Points` in a\n    :class: `PlanarDiagram` According to the following rules:\n\n    P_a,b P_b,c -> P_a,c\n    P_a,b P_a,b -> P_a,a\n    '''\n    import copy\n\n    pd_crossings = [x for x in planar_diagram if type(x) == Crossing]\n    pd_points = [x for x in planar_diagram if type(x) == Point]\n\n    pd_trimmed = PlanarDiagram()\n    for crossing in range(i+1, len(pd_points)):\n                    if (len(pd_trimmed[-1].components()) > 1 and\n                            len(pd_points[j].components()) > 1 and\n                            pd_points[j] not in substituted_points):\n                        # This skips P_a,a situations\n                        if pd_trimmed[-1].components() != pd_points[j].components():\n                            # This conditions skips (P_a,b P_a,b) situations\n                            substituted = False\n                            p = 0\n                            while substituted is not                # This deals with (P_a,b P_a,b) situations\n                            pd_trimmed.append(Point(pd_trimmed[-1].components()[0],\n                                                    pd_trimmed[-1].components()[0]))\n                            del pd_trimmed[-2]\n                            substituted_points.append(pd_points[j])\n                if pd_trimmed_old == Gauss diagram) and signs, both in dictionaries.'''\n\n    ## Arrow diagram for v_2 is\n    ## appear -> leave -> leave -> appear (with crossed arrows)\n    over_crossing_indices = {}\n    under_crossing_indices = {}\n    signs = {}\n    for i, row in enumerate(gc):\n        if row[1] == 1:\n            over_crossing_indices[row[0]] = i\n        else:\n crossed arrows)\n    over_crossing_indices = {}\n    under_crossing_indices = {}\n    signs = {}\n    for i, row in enumerate(gc):\n        if row[1] == 1:\n            over_crossing_indices[row[0]] = i\n        else:\n            under_crossing_indices[row[0]] = i\n        signs[row[0]] = row[2]\n\n    arrows = n.zeros((len(crossing_numbers), 3), dtype=n.long)\n\n    for index, number in pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    if not isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n\n    gc = representation._gauss_code\n    if len(gc) == 0:\n        return 0\n    elif len(gc) > 1:\n        raise Exception('tried to calculate second order writhe'\n                        'for something with more than 1 component')\n\n    gc = gc[0].copy()\n\n    arrows, signs = _crossing_arrows_and_signs(\n        gc, representation.crossing_numbers)\n    \n    crossing_numbers = list(representation.crossing_numbers)\n    result = 0\n    for index, i1 in enumerate(crossing_numbers):\n        arrow1 = arrows[i1]\n        a1s, a1e = arrow1\n        for i2 in crossing_numbers[index+1:]:\n            arrow2 = arrows[i2]\n            a2s, a2e = arrow2\n\n            if ((a2s > a1e and a1s > a2s and a2e > a1s) or\n                (a2e > a1e and a1s > a2e and a2s > a1s) or\n                (a2e > a1s and a1e > a2e and a2s > a1e)  result += signs[i1] * signs[i2]\n\n    return result\n\ndef arnold_2St_2Jplus(representation):\n    '''Returns J+ + 2 * St where J+ and St are Arnold's invariants of\n    plane curves.\n\n    The calculation is performed by transforming the representation\n    into a representation of an unknot by flipping crossings, then\n    calculating the second order writhe.\n\n    See 'Invariants of curves and fronts via Gauss diagrams', M\n    Polyak, Topology 37, 1998.\n\n    '''\n\n    from pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    if not isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n\n    gc = representation._gauss_code\n    if len(gc) == 0:\n        return 0\n    elif len(gc) > 1:\n        raise Exception('tried to calculate arnold_2St_2Jplus'\n                        'for something with more than 1 component')\n\n    gc = gc[0].copy()\n    seen_crossings = set()\n    changed_crossings = set()\n    for row in gc:\n        if row[0] not in seen_crossings:\n            seen_crossings.add(row[0])\n            if row[1] < 0:\n                changed_crossings.add(row[0])\n                row[1] *= -1\n                row[2] *= -1\n        elif row[0] in changed_crossings:\n            row[1] *= -1\n            row[2] *= -1\n    arrows, signs = _crossing_arrows_and_signs(\n        gc, representation.crossing_numbers)\n    \n    crossing_numbers = list(representation.crossing_numbers)\n    result = 0\n    for index, i1 in enumerate(crossing_numbers):\n        arrow1 = arrows[i1]\n        a1s, a1e = arrow1\n        for i2 in crossing_numbers[index+1:]:\n            arrow2 = arrows[i2]\n            a2s, a2e = arrow2\n\n            if ((a2s > a1e and a1s > a2s and a2e > a1s) or\n                (a2e > a1e and a1s > a2e and a2s > a1s) or\n                (a2e > a1s and a1e > a2e and a2s > a1e) or\n                (a2s > a1s and a1e > a2s and a2e > a1e)):\n            # if ((a2e > a1e and a1s > a2e and a2s > a1s) or\n            #     (a2s > a1s and a1e > a2s and a2e > a1e)):\n                result += signs[i1] * signs[i2]\n\n    return result\n\ndef Polyak, Topology 37, 1998.\n\n    '''\n    return arnold_2St_2Jplus(representation) - len(representation)\n\ndef vassiliev_degree_2(representation):\n    '''Calculates the Vassiliev invariant of degree 2 of the given\n    knot. The representation must have just one knot component,\n    this doesn't work for links.\n\n    Parameters\n    ==========\n    representation : Anything convertible to a\n                     :class:`~pyknotid.representations.gausscode.GaussCode`\n        A pyknotid representation class for the knot, or anything that\n        can automatically be converted into a GaussCode (i.e. by writing\n        :code:`GaussCode(your_object)`).\n    '''\n    ## See Polyak and Viro\n    from pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    if not isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n\n    gc = representation._gauss_code\n    if len(gc) == 0:\n        return 0\n    elif len(gc) > 1:\n        raise Exception('tried to calculate v2'\n                        'for something with more than 1 component')\n\n    gc = gc[0]\n    arrows, signs = _crossing_arrows_and_signs(\n        gc, representation.crossing_numbers)\n    \n    crossing_numbers = list(representation.crossing_numbers)\n    representations_sum = 0\n    for index, i1 in enumerate(crossing_numbers):\n        arrow1 = arrows[i1]\n        a1s, a1e = arrow1\n        for i2 in crossing_numbers[index+1:]:\n            arrow2 = arrows[i2]\n            a2s, a2e = arrow2\n\n            if a2s > a1s and a2e < a1s and a1e > a2s:\n                representations_sum += signs[i1] * signs[i2]\n            elif a1s > a2s and a1e < a2s and a2e > a1s:\n                representations_sum += signs[i1] * signs[i2]\n\n    return representations_sum\n\ndef vassiliev_degree_3(representation, try_cython=True):\n    '''Calculates the Vassiliev invariant of degree 3 of the given\n    knot. The representation must have just one knot component,\n    this doesn't work for links.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    representation : Anything convertible to a\n                     :class:`~pyknotid.representations.gausscode.GaussCode`\n        A pyknotid representation class for the knot, or anything that\n        can automatically be converted into a GaussCode (i.e. by writing\n        :code:`GaussCode(your_object)`).\n    try_cython : bool\n        Whether to try and use an optimised cython version of the\n        routine (takes about 1/3 of the time for complex representations).\n        Defaults to True, but the python fallback will be *slower*\n        than setting it to False if the cython function is not\n        available.\n    '''\n\n    if try_cython:\n        return _vassiliev_degree_3_numpy(representation)\n    return _vassiliev_degree_3_python(representation)\n\n        \ndef _vassiliev_degree_3_python(representation):\n    ## See Polyak and Viro\n    from pyknotid.representations.gausscode import GaussCode\n    if not isinstance(representation, GaussCode):\n        representation = GaussCode(representation)\n\n    gc = representation._gauss_code\n    if len(gc) == 0:\n        return 0\n    elif len(gc) > 1:\n        raise Exception('tried to calculate alexander polynomial'\n                        'for something with more than 1 component')\n\n    gc = gc[0]\n    arrows, signs = _crossing_arrows_and_signs(\n        gc, representation.crossing_numbers)\n    \n    crossing_numbers = list(representation.crossing_numbers)\n    used_sets = set()\n    representations_sum_1 = 0\n    representations_sum_2 = 0\n    for index, i1 in enumerate(crossing_numbers):\n        if index % 10 == 0:\n            vprint('\\rCurrently comparing index {}'.format(index), False)\n        arrow1 = arrows[i1]\n        a1s, a1e = arrow1\n        a1e = (a1e -
